A key's echo locally (22.6 ms) and its 95th percentile on both domains were within the local's budgets; what a key costs wasn't, 43 to 47 ms of the GUI thread and 259 to 429 messages for two frames.

### The whole suite after main's fixes came in

Main's perf/conductor-inbox (a frame draws and presents only the visuals whose content changed, the Conductor's view lays out and paints only when what it shows changes, the InMail thread watches its folders, a pane that hasn't printed for 2 s keeps its process list) and fix/mux-socket-guard came into this branch on 2026-10-30 (b51de71dd), and 1b80a3b11 then put the view's two timers through the counters. Three rounds of the whole suite after that, interleaved, against the budgets set before the merge: the merged build (20261030-111655-b51de71d, with 1b80a3b11's two lines) at 11:26 and 12:14, and the installed build (20261030-030621-0411a99a, main as it came in, which Lachlan's LBTerm runs; copied to a scratch folder, so no counters or metrics) at 11:49. The PC was unlocked and Lachlan at it; at each round's start no cargo or rustc ran, and 2 or 3 `lbterm-gui` (his daily one and other sessions' test windows) and 1 or 2 mux servers did. The merged build: 35 within, 8 over, 1 that couldn't be measured, then 33, 9 and 2; the installed build 36, 8 and 0. Each cell is the merged build's two rounds, then the installed build's; a `!` is over the budgets of then.

What main's fixes changed, against the rounds before the merge (which were run with the PC locked, so a time that moved by a few milliseconds may be the lock's; the comparison below is made in the same conditions):

- At rest the three over stayed over, each cheaper: a frame now draws only the visuals that changed. The Conductor's view with its seat sending still paints 4 frames a second (finding 12), at 28 to 40 ms a second of the GUI thread where it took 73 to 78; a blinking cursor 6 to 9 ms a second where it took 18 to 21, and the tasks card 5 to 7 where it took 17 to 20. What the server spent on their frames, 2.5 to 4.7 ms a second, is gone (finding 13): it was the process walk 4c705a05e stopped.
- A mux server at rest now spends 0.04 to 0.09 ms a second, where it spent nothing (finding 30).
- Actions on a mux domain no longer have the server walk the PC's processes: a tab switch's server CPU 0.45 to 0.7 ms where it was 12.5 to 13.8, a zoom's 3.2 to 4.4 where it was 57 to 88, a close's 16 to 23 where it was 38 to 50, a reload's 1.7 to 1.9 where it was 14.5 to 21.5, `cli list`'s 1.2 to 1.3 where it was 527 to 640 (finding 22). A tab switch by key shows in 7 to 10 ms where it took 13 to 19, the InMail list in 6 to 7 where it took 13 to 18, and the Conductor's view's opening costs the GUI thread 30 to 37 ms where it cost 99 to 121.
- A key's echo on a mux domain, 28 to 32 ms, is now within the local's time (finding 7), but what a key costs the GUI thread is still its wait: 26 to 30 ms and 345 to 468 messages on a mux domain, 20 to 24 ms and 87 to 155 locally (findings 1 and 23).
- Output, a cold start on a mux domain and the throughput's speed are as they were (findings 24 and 25): a writer's 16 MB show at 2.1 to 2.6 MB/s on a mux domain and 5.8 to 7.9 MB/s locally.

Six rounds of the throughputs alone followed (12:41 to 12:50: the build from before the merge, 20261029-213152-f9d36b5e, then the installed, then the merged, twice): on a mux domain 2.70 and 2.70 MB/s, 2.73 and 2.75, 2.73 and 2.72; locally 7.42 and 7.47, 7.71 and 7.84, 8.04 and 7.99. The PC unlocked, a local writer's 16 MB took 33.5 to 36.5 frames on all three builds, where the locked rounds' took 20 to 21 (finding 28).

The budgets were then set again from the merged build: its two suite rounds (and the four throughput rounds for the throughputs), by the same rule as before (one and a half times the slowest round's median or p95, with the floors), and where what was measured is the bug, what it should be:

- `key-echo`: held to the local's time, now 45 ms and a p95 of 65 (it was 35 and 80), and both key scenarios still to 10 ms of the GUI thread and 20 messages a key.
- `cli-list`: its server held to what this build costs, 3 ms; the bug it was held to (finding 22) is fixed. Its p95 budget is 530 ms, one and a half times one round's 352: that round's last run, whose call took 352 ms with the server spending 2 ms on it, the PC busy with Lachlan's work (the other rounds' p95s were 94 to 105). With fifteen runs the nearest-rank p95 is the slowest of them, so one slow call sets it. The first run after the warm-up still finds the panes' lists to take, 255 to 334 ms of the server's CPU in every round, which the median leaves out.
- `start` still to its tab within 1,900 ms, and `throughput` to the local's time (3,100 ms) and speed (at least 5.1 MB/s) and its GUI thread to the local's 2,200 ms.
- The servers' budgets for a tab switch, a close, a zoom, a reload and a resize are 3 to 35 ms now, where they were 20 to 140, so a process walk coming back fails them.

Do the new budgets catch what main's fixes took away? Three rounds of the scenarios over budget and every action but the new tabs and the throughputs, interleaved, 12:53 to 13:24, the PC unlocked: the build from before the merge (20261029-213152-f9d36b5e) at 12:53 and 13:15 (its second round cut off after `cli-list`), the merged build at 13:03. The old build is over in 17 of 19 and 15 of 16, on its process walks (a tab switch's server 13.5 to 14.3 ms against 3, a zoom's 54.5 to 56.4 against 7, `cli list`'s 547 and 666 against 3) and on its GUI thread (the rail's opening 44 ms against 40, the Conductor's view's 87 against 60). The merged build passes 10, with 7 over (the six known and one `split` whose p95 was 234 ms against 170, one run's: the GUI's own line said `split took 233.4 ms ... the rest 206.8 ms`, the GUI thread waiting) and 2 that couldn't be measured: a Teams prompt in Chrome and Earshot's tooltips appearing beside them (finding 32). Each cell is the old build's two rounds, then the merged build's.

### The whole suite on the branch's last builds

Three rounds of the whole suite against the budgets set from the merged build (above), interleaved, 14:39 to 15:46 on 2026-10-30: this branch's build (20261030-111655-b51de71d, the code of 629f2f7fd: the commits after the merge were scripts and docs) at 14:39 and 15:22, the installed build (20261030-030621-0411a99a) at 15:00. The PC was locked for the first two and unlocked from about 15:26 (DisplayFusion's and Chrome's windows from then on); at the first round's start two cargo and one rustc of another session's build ran, and 1 or 2 `lbterm-gui` (Lachlan's daily one, and another session's test window in the third). Every round: 37 within their budgets, 7 over, none that couldn't be measured (five scenarios in the third round and one in the first measured on their second try, after a foreign window or a pipe error the first time). The seven over are the seven held to what they should be: at rest `cursor-blink`, `conductor-view` and `tasks-card`; of the actions `key-echo` and `key-echo-local` (what a key costs the GUI thread and the messages it takes), `start` and `throughput`. Each cell is this branch's two rounds, then the installed build's; a `!` is over its budget.

Then main moved again: perf/frame-loop (bbb5c45af) fixed finding 1's WM_PAINT loop (a held WM_PAINT is validated, and the hold's end is a high-resolution timer in the message loop), paced frames to `max_fps` and never faster than the screen's refresh, stopped the IME's window being moved every frame (finding 8), and stopped the message loop draining its tasks while a key waited. It came into this branch at b07db1866, with the drawn cursor (fc83aaa4d, finding 34). Three rounds again, 16:05 to 17:22 on 2026-10-30: the merged build (20261030-155546-2653e849, built from b07db1866's tree as the merge was being made, so stamped with the commit before it) at 16:05 and 16:56, the installed build (20261030-030621-0411a99a) at 16:31. The PC was unlocked and Lachlan at it, working in Chrome; at the rounds' starts 3, 3 and 1 `lbterm-gui` ran (his daily one and other sessions' test windows), and at the third's 4 cargo and 4 rustc of another session's build. Each cell is the merged build's two rounds, then the installed build's; a `!` is over the budgets of then.

Against the budgets of then (set from the merged build before this merge), the merged build had 33 within, 8 over and 3 that couldn't be measured, then 34, 8 and 2; the installed build 37 and 7 over. What perf/frame-loop changed, the installed build's round beside it:

- A key no longer costs the GUI thread its wait: 9.9 to 11.7 ms and 6 to 7 messages a key on a mux domain, 9.3 to 9.7 ms and 5 to 6 locally, where the installed build spent 27.6 and 23.6 ms (findings 1 and 23, fixed). A key's echo shows in 22.2 to 23.2 ms on both domains, held there by the key's own frame (finding 35); the installed build's 30.4 and 31.0 ms go by the live cursor (finding 34), so they aren't the same measure.
- Every action costs the GUI thread less: a new tab 51 to 59 ms against 74, a split 57 to 59 against 77, a zoom 20 against 31, a resize 34 to 35 against 44, and each takes 7 to 18 messages where the WM_PAINT loop made it hundreds.
- At rest the three over cost the GUI thread as much as before and run fewer tasks and timers: a blinking cursor 5.1 to 5.8 tasks and 1.3 to 1.4 timers a second where it ran 9.1 to 9.9 and 2.4 (finding 8's IME task and the paint throttle's timer are gone), the tasks card 5.0 to 5.2 and 1.19 where it ran 8.4 and 2.1 to 2.2, the Conductor's view 6.1 and 0.38 where it ran 19.3 to 19.8 and 4.2.
- Output locally paints 61.5 to 63.5 frames for the 16 MB where it painted 34, and shows it in 2,179 ms (7.2 MB/s) against the installed build's 2,032 in the same rounds and this branch's 1,946 and 1,953 before the merge (finding 37). On a mux domain 2.66 and 2.72 MB/s, as before (finding 24).
- A tab switch by a key lost its frame three times (finding 36).

The action budgets were then set again from the merged build's two rounds by the same rule (one and a half times the slowest round, with floors, and where what was measured is the bug, what it should be). Most came down with the GUI thread's cost: a new tab's GUI thread from 130 ms to 90, a split's from 130 to 90, a zoom's from 60 to 35, the Conductor's view's opening from 60 to 45. A few went up a little: a local new tab's p95 from 130 to 150 and a split's from 170 to 190. The local output's frames went from 53 to 96, and the throughput's least from 5.1 to 4.8 MB/s and its time from 3,100 to 3,300 ms; `cli list`'s p95 from 530 to 170. Both key scenarios are held to 20 ms, p95 35, for what an echo should take (finding 35).

Judged again against those: the merged build 34 within, 7 over and 3 that couldn't be measured, then 35, 7 and 2; the installed build 37 within and 7 over. Over on the merged build: at rest `cursor-blink`, `conductor-view` and `tasks-card` (findings 14, 12 and 15) in both rounds, and `four-windows` in the first (2.25 tasks a second against 2: the slow log's waker ran 31 times over its spans; 1.19 in the second round); of the actions `key-echo` and `key-echo-local` (finding 35), `start` (finding 25) and `throughput` (finding 24). Couldn't be measured: five scenarios for windows of Lachlan's Chrome appearing beside them (popups, menus, its extensions page; finding 32), and `tab-by-key` twice for its lost frame (finding 36). On the installed build the same seven were over, and both keys for their GUI thread too.

Main moved once more before the check: perf/alerts (a title, a status report or a palette costs only what it changed) and feat/tab-switch-fade (a tab switch fades like a close) came in at 7c87e8a8c. One round of the whole suite on that build (20261030-174505-7c87e8a8), 17:53 to 18:21 on 2026-10-30, against the budgets above, with Lachlan at the PC (another session's two cargo and one rustc at its start): 33 within, 6 over, 5 that couldn't be measured. Over: at rest `cursor-blink`, `conductor-view` and `tasks-card`; of the actions `key-echo-local` (22.1 ms against 20, finding 35), `start` (its pane at 2,773 ms against 1,900, finding 25) and `throughput` (2.78 MB/s against at least 4.8, finding 24). Couldn't be measured, each tried twice: `four-windows`, `key-echo` (over as well: 25.2 ms, p95 43, the GUI thread 13 ms), `tab-by-key`, `tab-by-click` and `reload`, every one for windows of Lachlan's own programs appearing beside it (finding 32); none for a lost frame this time. The fade left a tab switch's time where it was (6.8 ms by key, 8.6 by a click), and perf/alerts took a reload to one frame and no server CPU, a new tab's GUI thread to 44 ms and a split's to 42. A `!` is over its budget.

### A key's echo, after perf/key-latency

Findings 35 and 68 fixed: a key typed into a pane asks for no frame of its own unless that frame draws something new, and on a mux domain the server's answer to a key, which changes nothing before the echo, asks for no frame either. The measurements below were made with the branch's own fix for finding 67 too (the parser's wait for the rest of a frame ending when it's due), which main has had since 01659fc5d (perf/mux-server's `lbterm_wait.rs`, which replaced the branch's): so against a build from 01659fc5d on, what this branch takes off an echo is findings 35 and 68's alone, and that build's echo still waits behind the key's own frame (below, "Against main's build"). Two rounds of every key scenario and the load and throughput ones, interleaved, 06:21 to 06:58 on 2026-10-31: the installed build (20261030-191144-301f18e6, a copy in a scratch folder; it has no `drawn_cursor`, so its keys go by the live cursor, which takes an echo that came while a frame was drawn for that frame's, finding 34), stage 1's build of this branch (a63cdffd1, the process walk gone, finding 49) and this build (9d2e5f740's code). The PC was locked throughout; at the runs' starts 1 to 4 `lbterm-gui` ran (other sessions' test windows) and in some another session's build (up to 2 cargo and 5 rustc). Each cell is a round's median over its 15 runs (6 for a throughput), the two rounds side by side; for the bursts and the held keys, the median of the runs' median echo and the 95th percentile of each run's slowest.

| Scenario | installed | stage 1 | this build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `key-echo`, to the frame | 22.2, 22.2 | 22.1, 22.3 | 10.4, 9.8 |
| its p95 | 27.6, 25.9 | 23.1, 42.7 | 12.4, 17.3 |
| its GUI thread, frames | 9.5, 9.4; 2 | 9.9, 10.7; 2 | 5.6, 6.4; 1 |
| `key-echo-local`, to the frame | 22.4, 22.7 | 22.7, 22.3 | 8.9, 9.4 |
| its p95 | 24.2, 25.1 | 24.2, 24.5 | 11.4, 15 |
| its GUI thread, frames | 9.1, 9; 2 | 9.4, 10.3; 2 | 5.1, 6.3; 1 |
| `key-burst`, each echo | 28.5, 27.6 | 27.7, 28.5 | 17.5, 17.6 |
| its slowest | 37.6, 38 | 37.5, 40.8 | 26.1, 26 |
| `key-burst-local`, each echo | 13.9, 15.6 | 23.8, 27.4 | 16.3, 16.6 |
| its slowest | 37, 35.5 | 37.5, 37.7 | 25.7, 25 |

| its slowest | 41, 42.8 | 55.7, 55.6 | 23.9, 21.2 |

| its slowest | 28.3, 27.4 | 57.4, 32.8 | 21.3, 20 |

| `throughput-local`, MB/s | 7.61, 7.59 | 7.27, 7.27 | 7.89, 7.8 |

- **One key** shows in the first frame after it, in 8.9 to 10.4 ms, where every echo of stage 1's but one in 60 came in the second (one the third), at 21.1 ms or more: the frame starts painting 4.6 and 4.7 ms after the key locally and 5.7 and 5.7 on a mux domain (`present`'s `paint_ms`), against 17.5 to 17.7, and its paint and presents take about 4.5 ms more. The installed build's are stage 1's but for the mux server's process walk (finding 49): 14.9 to 15.8 ms of the server a key against 1.7 to 2.1.
- **A burst** of keys faster than a refresh shares frames paced to the 60 Hz screen: each key falls about 1.7 ms further behind the last (16.7 ms frames against 15 ms keys), so the ten echoes run from 7.5 to 26.1 ms, a median of 16.3 to 17.6, where stage 1's took 23.8 to 28.5 at the median and 37.5 to 40.8 at the slowest. On Lachlan's 160 Hz main screen frames are 8.3 ms apart and a burst this fast doesn't fall behind. The installed build's local 13.9 and 15.6 are the live cursor's (finding 34).
- **A key held** at Lachlan's repeat rate (33.3 ms, two refreshes apart) has every echo in a frame of its own: 9.4 ms on a mux domain and 8.5 locally, against 22.3 to 22.7.
- **Under load** on a mux domain the echo shows in 27.1 and 30.1 ms against 35.3 to 36.5. Locally it's no better: in four more rounds (07:00 to 07:03) stage 1's 57.9, 53.6, 63.3 and 64.2 against this build's 62.7, 66.7, 50.6 and 60.2, the GUI's own times 47 to 48 against 45 to 47. What holds it is finding 44's: the key's wait behind the writer's frames and the paint behind the writer's terminal.
- **Output.** Locally the 16 MB showed at 7.89 and 7.8 MB/s against stage 1's 7.27 and 7.27, the GUI thread spending 1,256 and 1,253 ms against 1,378 and 1,362: the parser's wait ends at 3 ms, not on the tick (finding 67), which takes back finding 37's loss of speed, though not its frames (61 against its budget of 53). On a mux domain the same within the rounds' spread (finding 24); this build's first round ran beside another session's build.
- **The key's own frame**, whether a key's echo can show sooner: no frame can draw the echo before its output arrives, and its frame now starts then. What's left is the paint (about 4.5 ms of pass, draw and present), the output's own way to the GUI (the program's echo through ConPTY, the parser's 3 ms wait, which `mux_output_parser_coalesce_delay_ms = 0` would take off: 6.2 ms locally with it), and after the frame, DWM's next composition.

**The swapchain and DWM's clock.** Every visual's swapchain is a DirectComposition one, FLIP_DISCARD with three buffers, presented Fifo (SyncInterval 1) with a frame latency of 2 (`termwindow/webgpu.rs`; wgpu-hal's dx12 `acquire_texture` waits on the frame-latency object, for up to a second). For a key at rest none of it adds anything: nothing is queued, so the acquire doesn't wait, and the present goes up at DWM's next composition, which neither a latency of 1 nor Mailbox nor a wait on DWM's clock can bring sooner. The acquire wasn't seen waiting at all: with compositor.rs's debug line on (it's said when preparing and acquiring a frame's visuals take over 8 ms together), `throughput-local` (paced frames back to back), `key-echo-load-local` and `key-echo` said it only for each window's first frames, whose 74 to 138 ms went on making the swapchains, their acquires 0.09 to 0.36 ms. Paced frames never get ahead of the screen (a period is never shorter than a refresh), so the queue holds at most the one frame DWM hasn't composed yet. `DCompositionWaitForCompositorClock` would buy an echo at rest nothing: waiting on the clock could only start its frame later. What it could buy is an even cadence for frames back to back, started just after DWM's tick instead of a period from the last paint's start: on Lachlan's main screen (159.952 Hz) `max_fps` 120 paces streaming output at 8.33 ms against refreshes 6.25 ms apart, so, by the arithmetic and not measured, its frames go up one, one and two refreshes apart in turn, which pacing to the clock, every refresh or every other, would even out. That's how output looks as it streams, not how soon a key shows, and none of it can be seen here: the test windows are hidden on a 60 Hz screen (finding 39) and the PC was locked. Nothing about the swapchain was changed. *Under load it's not known* (the review of 2026-10-31): with a writer's frames going up every refresh, an echo's frame can queue behind one DWM hasn't composed yet (Fifo, a latency of 2), and pacing to DWM's clock might put it up a refresh sooner; what would say so is how many echoes' frames went up a refresh late, from the screen's own timing. Tried on 2026-11-01, unlocked, two rounds interleaved (16:06 to 16:08) of `key-echo-load` and `key-echo-load-local` (the runner's `LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING`, the window moved clear where it can be): the right-most screen was covered whole by Lachlan's own windows (Direction's box and Discord, 100% each), so every echo's frame was hidden (`screen_hidden`) and no screen time could be read. What the GUI says: on a mux domain an echo 21.3 and 19.9 ms at the median on this branch against 20.7 and 16.4 on the installed build (20261101-134606-4afd2a40), its frame's paint starting 17.2 and 11.0 ms after the key against 17.3 and 9.9, the echo in the second to fourth frame after its key (the writer's frames between); locally 46.0 and 38.4 against 45.4 and 38.0, the paint starting 17.4 ms after the key on both. So the branch's fixes buy an echo nothing under load either way, and whether DWM's clock would is still to measure, with the right-most screen clear (finding 44).

### A cold start, after perf/key-latency

Finding 25 fixed: a GUI starting on its mux domain starts the server at once when no server holds the socket, and tries the new server's socket every 10 ms (lbterm/MUX.md, "Starting the server"). Two rounds of `start` and `start-local`, interleaved, 08:11 to 08:16 on 2026-10-31: the installed build (20261030-191144-301f18e6, a copy in a scratch folder), stage 2's build of this branch (9d2e5f740's code) and this build (82958fb8c). The PC was locked throughout; at each scenario's start 1 to 4 `lbterm-gui` and 1 or 2 `lbterm-mux-server` of other sessions' tests ran, and no cargo or rustc. Each cell is a round's median over its 6 runs after a warm-up, the two rounds side by side; a start's costs are from nothing to the window quiet.

| | installed | stage 2 | this build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `start`, to its tab's pane | 2,757, 2,761 | 2,752, 2,752 | 1,046, 1,060 |
| its slowest | 2,853, 2,785 | 2,766, 2,829 | 1,219, 1,236 |
| its first frame | 1,142, 876 | 899, 953 | 893, 906 |
| its frames | 12.5, 12 | 12, 12.5 | 6, 6 |
| its GUI thread, ms | 1,003, 938 | 967, 1,025 | 886, 890 |
| its mux server, ms | 150, 153 | 121, 119 | 118, 126 |
| `start-local`, to its tab | 948, 943 | 937, 962 | 960, 906 |

- **The tab on a mux domain** is up 1.05 s after its process starts, where it took 2.75 s: 100 to 150 ms after a local start's, and after the window's own first frame. The GUI starts the server 82 to 87 ms after its process starts and it answers 116 to 167 ms later (the GUI's `lbterm::serve` lines in `perf/server-start.ps1` and these runs), where upstream tried the socket ten times over 2.25 s first and then waited 200 ms before trying the server it had started. Its `ready_ms` was within `start`'s budget of then (1,900 ms, the local's and 300 ms for the server) in every run, its slowest 1,236, and budgets.json now holds it to the local's own, 1,600 ms (one and a half times this build's slowest round), and its frames to 9 where they were allowed 20.
- **What's left** is the 140 to 180 ms from the first frame to the server's pane. The connection is up 0.35 to 0.6 s before the GUI's state file is opened (its log), so it's the attach's own steps (the version check, the list of panes, the window's tabs), which wait for the GUI thread while it makes the window; not looked into further.
- **Half the frames**: the window shows its "Connecting..." pane for about 150 ms, not 1.9 s, and its GUI thread spends 50 to 100 ms less. The mux server's CPU is stage 2's (finding 49 took the installed build's process walk out of it).

### Output, after perf/key-latency

Finding 37 fixed, with finding 69 found behind it: a frame copies a local pane's rows out of its terminal and lays them out with it let go (`render/pane_lines.rs`), and the scroll strip's prompt marks read a row off its runs of attributes (`Line::has_semantic_type`). What a frame spent with the terminal held, timed in two probe builds of this branch (main's perf/at-rest merged in, histograms around each part of a frame and each step of the parser, since taken out), one run of `throughput-local` each, 09:16 and 09:30 on 2026-10-31, the PC locked; medians a frame, or a 128 KB batch for the parser:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| a frame's pass (`gui.paint.impl`) | 28.2 ms | 13.7 ms |
| the pane's rows laid out | 3.8 ms, the terminal held | 3.5 ms, the terminal free; the copy 0.2 ms held |
| the implicit hyperlinks | 1.4 ms, held | 1.3 ms, held (finding 70) |
| the strip's prompt marks | 11.7 ms, held, all of it CPU | 0.6 ms |
| the rail | 8.4 ms, 1.6 of it CPU, 6.8 waiting for the terminal | 5.3 ms, 1.5 of it CPU, 3.8 waiting (finding 70) |
| the parser: applying a batch, parsing it | 7.3 ms, 2.5 ms | 7.1 ms, 2.6 ms |
| the parser waiting for the terminal | 6.7 ms at the mean, 3.7 at the median, 16 at the 95th percentile | 0, 0.2 at the 95th percentile |
| the parser waiting for output from the reader | 0 at the median | 0 at the median |

With the probes' other builds: with the rows copied but the marks as they were, output was no faster (6.9 MB/s, the parser still waiting on the marks); with the marks left out but the rows laid out in the lock, 9.5 MB/s; with both, 12.0. Then two rounds of `throughput-local` and `throughput`, interleaved, 09:45 to 09:53 on 2026-10-31: the installed build (20261030-191144-301f18e6, a copy in a scratch folder), this branch before the fix (114d7b337, main's perf/at-rest merged in, with the first probes' histograms in it) and this build (24833f029's code). The PC was locked; at the runs' starts 1 to 4 `lbterm-gui` of other sessions' tests ran, and at some another session's build (up to 4 cargo and 8 rustc). Each cell is a round's median over its 6 runs after a warm-up, the two rounds side by side:

| | installed | before | this build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `throughput-local`, MB/s | 7.62, 7.59 | 7.68, 7.75 | 12.08, 12.37 |
| its time to the frame, ms | 2,058, 2,066.5 | 2,042.6, 2,024.7 | 1,299.2, 1,268.4 |
| its GUI thread, ms | 1,283.7, 1,289.6 | 1,282.2, 1,273.5 | 652.9, 633.4 |
| its process, ms | 2,581.3, 2,574.4 | 2,593.6, 2,585.6 | 1,960.8, 1,881.1 |
| its frames | 61.5, 64 | 64, 62.5 | 58, 60.5 |
| the writer's own writes, ms | 1,298, 1,289.5 | 1,272, 1,269.5 | 827, 799.5 |
| the parser's batch from its start to the terminal let go, mean ms | (no metrics) | 12.9, 12.9 | 7.5, 7.3 |
| `throughput` (a mux domain), MB/s | 2.72, 2.62 | 2.82, 2.72 | 2.83, 2.74 |

- **The parser is the limit now.** Its thread spends 1.22 to 1.33 s of CPU on the 16 MB on every build; before, it took 2 s of wall time to do it, a third of that waiting for the frames. What's left is its own work, the terminal's model taking in each batch (7.1 ms a 128 KB) and the parsing (2.6 ms): 12 MB/s, and the writer finishes its writes in 0.8 s where it took 1.3, ConPTY no longer held up behind LBTerm's reading.
- **frame-loop's gains are kept.** Frames come as each paints, 58 to 60.5 for the 16 MB, about 46 a second here, where the pacing held none (each paint longer than a refresh) and now holds about 2 a run; the GUI thread doesn't go round its loop between them (66 to 107 turns a run); a window that can't be seen still paints once a second (`work.rs`, untouched).
- **On a mux domain** output is as it was (finding 24): the server parses it, and the GUI paints 3 or 4 frames for the 16 MB, so its frames never held a parser up. The GUI's copy is only of a local pane's rows: a mux domain's pane draws from the client's own lines, which no parser holds.
- **What a frame still waits for** is the parser: reading the rail's tabs and copying the rows each wait for the batch it's applying, 3.8 ms a frame at the median (finding 70). That's the GUI thread's time, not output's speed.
- **A frame that changes few rows copies few.** Copying every row anew cost most where a frame changes least: a row of 24-bit colours keeps each cell's attributes on the heap, so a screen of 160 by 60 cells each its own colour, a spinner rewriting one row every 100 ms, held the terminal 0.91 to 0.99 ms a frame for the copy. A row the last frame copied that's equal to the terminal's (Line's equality: its sequence number, its bits, its cells) is taken from there now, so that frame copies one row and compares the rest: 0.50 and 0.59 ms (a probe, two rounds interleaved, 11:13 to 11:15). It's the copy's worst case, and still costs a frame there 0.5 to 1 ms more than laying the rows out in the lock, which costs an unchanged row nothing (its pass 7.3 and 7.7 ms against 6.3 and 6.8); the compare is what's left of it (finding 70). A frame that changes no row (the keyboard taken away and given back) copies none anew (`perf/output-hold.ps1`).
- **ConPTY 1.25**, which main's chore/conpty-1.25 bundles in place of 1.22 and which came into this branch at 58e415259, shows the 16 MB as 1.22 does: 1,270.5 ms (12.35 MB/s) in the round that had the PC to itself (11:16), against 1,268 to 1,299 with 1.22. Its other rounds ran beside another session's build and up to five other test windows, and differed from round to round by more than the two ConPTYs ever did.
- **With main's perf/mux-server merged in** (d6892478f, 2026-10-31, 13:33 to 13:45, the PC unlocked and idle, 1 to 5 other test windows beside it): the 16 MB showed locally in 1,291 to 1,313 ms (five runs; 11.85 to 12.09 MB/s), against 1,270 to 1,300 for this branch's build before the merge (four runs) and 2,071 and 2,088 for the installed build (7.46 and 7.51 MB/s), the GUI thread 626 to 640 ms against 623 to 634 and 1,285 to 1,306. The writer was done later, at 975 to 995 ms against 795 to 828: perf/mux-server reads ConPTY's output into 128 KB where upstream read into a megabyte, and a probe build with the megabyte back had the writer done at 817 and 818 ms but the 16 MB shown no sooner (1,300 and 1,309 ms), so the 1 or 2% the merge may have cost isn't the read's. On a mux domain the merge made output faster, 3.52 and 3.91 MB/s (4,012 and 4,111 ms) against 2.71 and 2.79 before it and 2.65 and 2.69 for the installed build, its GUI thread spending 3,224 and 3,330 ms against 2,467 and 2,576 (finding 24).
- **With main's feat/switch-calls merged in too** (b08366771, 14:12 to 14:20, 4 to 6 other sessions' test windows and 2 to 4 of their mux servers beside it): the 16 MB showed locally in 1,264 ms and 1,294 ms in the runs the PC let it, its GUI thread 516 and 522 ms (626 to 640 before that merge, which made the line caches four times the size: not looked into), against 2,065 to 2,168 for the installed build. Three runs between them took 1,388, 2,011 and 2,063 ms with every thread of the window spending about half as much again, the update checker's idle thread too (42 and 59 ms against 25 to 27), the writer done at 1,524 and 1,566 ms: the PC slower for everything then, not the window waiting. The installed build, bound by its frames' holds rather than by its CPU, moved less (2,109 and 2,168). On a mux domain 3.18 MB/s in the first round (4,577 ms) and 2.46 in that slower stretch, against 2.70 and 1.91 for the installed build.

- **This branch**: 39 within, 10 over and 2 that couldn't be measured, then 46, 5 and 0. Over in both rounds: at rest `cursor-blink` (finding 14) and `conductor-view` (finding 12), and `throughput` on a mux domain (2.47 and 2.6 MB/s, finding 24). Over in one: in the first round, which ran beside another session's build, `throughput-local` (10.93 MB/s against its least of 11.5; 12.28 in the second), `projects-card` at rest (one stray frame, 3.56 ms a second of the GUI thread), `key-echo`'s server (2.24 ms a key against 2), `key-burst`'s echoes (20.3 ms against 20), `key-held-local`'s slowest (35.1 against 35), `new-tab` (62.1 ms against 60) and `new-tab-local`'s p95 (163 against 150); in the second, `key-echo-local`'s p95 (29 ms against 23) and `tab-by-key`'s (39.9 against 20). Couldn't be measured: `key-held` (the bench session's Windows Terminal windows) and `reload` (Chrome's, 32 ms after Lachlan's input).
- **The installed build**: 38 within, 13 over, then 35, 13 and 3 that couldn't be measured (Chrome's windows). Over in both: `cursor-blink`, `conductor-view` and `tasks-card` at rest (finding 15, fixed by main's perf/at-rest), every key scenario but under load (findings 35, 49, 67 and 68: an echo 22.2 to 24.7 ms against this branch's 9.3 to 14.5, the server 19.4 and 22.8 ms a key against 1.83 and 2.24, a held key two frames a repeat), `start` (its tab at 2,817 and 2,959 ms, finding 25), `throughput` and `throughput-local` (7.39 and 7.55 MB/s, finding 37).
- What this branch fixed shows in every round: the keys, the start and the local output pass, or miss their budgets by a millisecond or two, where the installed build fails them by half again or more; at rest nothing moved but `tasks-card`, main's. The over-by-a-little on this branch came with load from beside it (the first round), and each passed in the other round.

### The whole suite on perf/fonts-core

Two rounds of every scenario, interleaved, on 2026-11-01: the installed build (main at 7f67863f, stamped 20261101-154550-7f67863f, before perf/key-latency landed; a copy in a scratch folder) at 18:28 and 19:49, and this branch merged with main as far as perf/key-latency and perf/bench-terminals (9af4029ec, stamped 20261101-175051-9af4029e) at 19:10 and 20:37, each round 39 to 47 minutes; the tables give them in the order they ran: installed, this branch, installed, this branch. The PC was unlocked at every round's start and end and Lachlan was at it (his Chrome windows came up in five window checks, each scenario run again); another session's budget scenarios and test windows (perf/gpu-memory's) ran beside the last three rounds and a build beside the first. So what tells the two builds apart here is main's perf/key-latency (keys, a mux domain's cold start, local output) as well as this branch; "Against main's own build", below, takes main's part out.

- **This branch**: 69 within, 15 over and none that couldn't be measured, then 74, 9 and 1 (`resize`, a Chrome window of Lachlan's). Over in both rounds: at rest `cursor-blink` (4.56 and 4.53 tasks a second against 4, finding 14) and `conductor-view` (finding 12); `busy-scroll` and `busy-triggers-scroll` (main's budgets, which the installed build is over in both rounds too: `task_ms` 79.7 to 120 ms a second against 46 and 48); and four frames budgets this branch had set before the merge, `throughput-unicode`, `throughput-rgb`, `throughput-rgb-local` and `scrollback`, which counted 4 or 5 frames on a mux domain while each frame fetched the scrollback for the prompt marks: since main's perf/lbterm-layers and perf/key-latency a frame comes for each refresh the output shows at (68 to 211.5 on a mux domain), so they're set again from these rounds (budgets.json says each). Over in one: `busy-one-tab`, `busy-tabs` and `busy-rail-open` in the first (their `frame_ms` 3.55 to 4.15 against 2.5 to 3.1; the installed build over in both, 4.48 to 5.2), `tab-by-click`'s, `zoom`'s and `reload`'s p95 and `font-zoom` (341 ms against 330) in the first, `key-echo-load`'s p95 in the second (184 ms against 90).
- **The installed build**: 46 within and 38 over, then 48, 35 and 1. Over in both, beside the above: the key scenarios but under load, `key-burst-local` in one (findings 35, 49, 67 and 68, fixed by perf/key-latency), `start`'s `ready_ms` (finding 25, the same), `throughput-local` and `scrollback-local` (5.0 to 6.9 MB a second), `throughput-rgb-local` (2.0 and 2.3 MB a second), `reload` (285 and 318 ms) and `fallback-line` (125 and 127 ms), and most of the `busy-` scenarios.
- **Before the merge** (2026-10-31, 13:20 to 15:48: this branch at c9852a97 against the build installed then, 20261030-191144-301f18e6, each twice in turn): this branch 45 within, 9 over and 3 errors (`new-tab` and `new-tab-local`, finding 92, and `split`, a window in the window check), then 49, 8 and 0; the installed build 42, 15 and 0, then 31, 19 and 7 (all seven windows in the window check: Lachlan's Chrome, Explorer's copy dialogs, an ImageView). What this branch fixed showed there the same: `reload` 81 and 109 ms against 241 and 309, `start-local`'s GUI thread 500 and 509 ms against 1,118 and 1,224, `throughput-local` 726 and 704 ms against 2,055 and 2,583, `throughput-rgb-local` 3,461 and 3,634 ms against 10,081 and 7,059, `scrollback-local` 364 and 350 ms against 976 and 1,100 (its GUI's private memory 539 and 546 MB against 583 and 591).

### Against main's own build

The installed build above lacks perf/key-latency, which this branch's merge brought in, so the scenarios this branch's stages changed were run again against main's own build, main at 6cc0bc7ef (perf/key-latency landed; stamped 20261101-174015-6cc0bc7e), and this branch at 9af4029ec, the same two builds but for this branch's changes and perf/bench-terminals' scripts: two rounds of each interleaved, main first, on 2026-11-01 21:32 to 22:02, through the warden (`budgets -Only`, fifteen scenarios), the PC unlocked. Another session ran the same builds from this slot at the same time (main 21:26 to 21:39, this branch 21:40 to 21:52, 25 scenarios), which overlapped all but this branch's second round; its numbers agree with these (this branch's `reload` 117 ms, `font-zoom` 285, `throughput-local` 373, `scrollback-local` 241; main's 346, 511, 2,511 and 1,149). - **Printing** (stage 2): 16 MB of plain lines locally 365 and 416 ms against 1,858 and 2,409 (37.7 and 43 MB a second against 6.5 and 8.5), on a mux domain 250 and 255 against 1,168 and 1,181 (61.5 and 64.9 MB a second against 13.3 and 13.4, the server spending 250 and 284 ms of CPU against 1,274 and 1,285), Claude Code's look locally 4,170 and 4,724 ms against 10,423 and 10,986, and 100,000 lines into a full scrollback locally 178 and 206 ms against 1,069 and 1,127, the GUI holding 532 and 533 MB against 572 and 573. Wide characters and emoji locally show no difference past the runs' spread (3,518 and 4,726 ms against 4,295 and 4,579; finding 85).
- **The caches and upstream's metrics** (stage 3): `repaint`, a frame of two panes of lines the window has drawn, 1.84 and 2.22 ms against 2.19 and 2.78, its GUI thread 2.28 and 2.5 ms against 2.47 and 3.19. `busy-one-tab` and `busy-scroll` show no difference past the spread.
- **Worse**: `key-echo-load`, a key's echo on a mux domain with a writer at full speed beside it, at the median 28 ms against 24.9 and 26.3, but over its p95 budget in both rounds (174 and 145 ms against 44 and 123), its GUI thread 334 and 337 ms against 207 and 208: finding 93.

### Against main's build of 2026-11-03, by `budgets -Against`

The scenarios this branch's stages changed, once main's budget runner of 2026-11-03 (feat/budgets-busy-pc: the PC watched, CPU at the nominal clock, Welch's test on the rounds; "Two builds against each other", above) and main's merges since 2026-11-01 had come in: `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only reload,font-zoom,new-window,fallback-line,start-local,repaint,throughput-local,throughput-unicode-local,throughput-rgb-local,scrollback-local -Against <installed copy> -Rounds 5`, 2026-11-03 19:58 to 20:51 in a warden GPU job, the PC unlocked throughout (Lachlan at it: Chrome's and Discord's windows took five rounds' first tries, each taken again), others on 13 to 31% of the test's CPUs, no span or run busy. Before: a copy of the installed build, main at de9558132 (20261103-011219-de955813). After: this branch at its merge of main (ee9d324f1, 20261103-190000-ee9d324f), which the commits after it change only where a reload makes the fonts again (`forget_missing`), the docs and the budgets. Each cell is the two builds' medians over their rounds, main's first, then the difference's verdict and its +-99% half width; a time is to the frame, the CPU at the nominal clock. Every scenario passed on this branch's build against the budgets of then; main's build was over in reload, font-zoom, throughput-local (11.2 MB/s against at least 11.5), throughput-rgb-local and scrollback-local.

Of 63 metrics the test told apart, 58 were lower and 5 higher: the three throughputs' MB/s, `font-zoom`'s window messages (two more a zoom) and `fallback-line`'s longest paint while its motion played, 10.8 ms longer. That paint wasn't looked into; this branch hands a fallback's face to the font and shapes the window again in one pass, where main threw the pass away part-way (`ClearShapeCache`) and shaped again in the next, which would put the work of two paints in one, but that's a reading of the code, not a measurement. The time to its frame was 32 ms sooner and the window quiet no later. Its counts on this branch's build, every round: a reload 0 fonts made, 0 files read, 0 searches, 0 atlases and 0 reshapes; a zoom 4 fonts, 1 atlas, 1 reshape and no search; a new window 4 fonts and 1 atlas; a fallback line 1 search and 2 reshapes.

A start's first frame came about 160 ms sooner (755 to 782 ms against 909 to 963; the rounds' means 162 ms apart, the 99% interval 122 to 202), its GUI thread 324 ms lighter and the process's CPU no different: unlike the rounds that took the start apart on 2026-10-31 ("A window's start", above), where the first frame came no sooner with the warm-up than without. That's this branch's build against main's as wholes, so which change it's owed to (the warm-up, the font setup, wgpu's indirect checks off, the first atlas) isn't known.

Main's build showed `throughput-local` at 10.95 to 11.53 MB/s (1,362 to 1,433 ms for the 16 MB), about the 11.5 of its budget and the 1.3 s of its README line, where its builds of 2026-11-01 showed half that (finding 94).

These rounds set the budgets of `reload` (whose 130 ms, from two locked rounds, failed an unlocked one beside other tracks' test windows; the review of this branch), `throughput-unicode-local` (whose 7,300 ms came from a round beside another session's build), `throughput-local` (main's, from perf/key-latency's 1.3 s), `throughput-rgb-local` and `scrollback-local`, each 1.5 times this build's slowest round, its least MB/s the slowest round's over 1.5.

### The menus' fade, by `budgets -Against` (feat/new-tab-menus, 2026-11-05)

NEW-TAB.md's landing 2 (every menu fading on `pane_enter` and `pane_exit`, its highlight gliding, one falloff for its light, the tab menu's facts gathered as it opens) against main's build: `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,inbox-opening,prompts-opening,conductor-view-opening,rail-opening,key-echo,one-tab -Bin <a copy of the branch's build> -Against <a copy of the installed build> -Rounds 5`, 2026-11-05 17:49 to 18:31 in a warden GPU job with the lock, the PC locked throughout (LogonUI), others on 12 to 22% of the test's CPUs and no span or run busy; the stage's e2e scripts ran beside it in a GPU job of their own. Before: the installed build, main at f9f790489 (20261105-130213-f9f79048), whose Rust is main's of then. After: the branch at ce16c74bf's Rust and stage 6's copy routing (20261105-173026-ce16c74b; stage 6's change is one call in `copy_to_clipboard`, which nothing measured copies through). `tab-menu-opening` is new with this branch: a right click on the first of twelve tabs' pads, the pointer resting on it, to the frame with the menu. Medians over the rounds, main's first, then the verdict and its +-99% half width:

- At rest (`one-tab`): GUI CPU 1.53 against 1.57 ms a second, same (+-0.126); the server 0.061 against 0.062, same; private memory 383 and 383 MB, same; handles 817 against 812, same by Welch's (+-15.2), lower in 5 of 5 rounds paired (+-3.12, not a verdict).
- `key-echo`: 8.27 against 7.77 ms, same (+-2.2); its frame the first, 1 frame and 3 messages each; GUI thread 2.28 against 2.53 ms, same (+-0.744).
- `rail-opening`: 162 against 164 ms, same (+-3.73); GUI thread 5.71 against 6.33, same (+-1.43); 4 frames each.
- `conductor-view-opening`: 7.69 against 6.69 ms, same (+-1.67); GUI thread 14.8 against 14.9, same; 4 frames each.
- `inbox-opening`: 3.23 against 2.85 ms, same (+-0.979); GUI thread 4.38 against 4.27, same; 2 frames each; settled 151 against 255 ms, higher (+-3.75): the list now fades in.
- `prompts-opening`: 24.6 against 23.8 ms, same (+-3.66), its prompts in the frame after the click's, as before; settled 24.6 against 266 ms, higher; frames 2 against 3 and messages 7 against 8, higher; GUI thread 10.2 against 21.3 ms, higher (+-4.61), and CPU 12.5 against 24.9, higher (+-5.43).
- `tab-menu-opening`: 1.91 against 1.65 ms, same (+-0.629), the menu in the frame after the click in every run of both; settled 1.91 against 256 ms, higher; frames 1 against 2 and messages 3 against 4, higher; GUI thread 3.34 against 4.35 ms, same (+-42.1: one round of the branch's took 49.9 ms a run, the other four 2.95 to 5.56).

What the higher ones are. Every menu now fades in on `pane_enter`, so it settles about 250 ms after it shows, where it was whole in its first frame: the menu shows no later (`ms` and `shown_frame` the same), and DWM plays the fade with no frame of the GUI's. The frame and message more is the state file's own, asked 20 ms after the last animation in flight ends so its snapshot can say `animating: false` (lbterm/STATE.md, "The frame an animation ends on"; termwindow/state.rs, `lbterm_state_frame`): a window without the state file, Lachlan's, doesn't paint it. In `prompts-opening` that frame costs a whole layout of the list again, 10 ms or so of the GUI thread, because the prompt list lays out and shapes its 40 rows on every paint it's in (What the cards cost, what's left); the tab menu's and the InMail list's frames cost a millisecond or so. Read from the code and the frame counts, not a profile: in Lachlan's window the prompt list's opening costs what it did, and any paint while it's open (output in the pane under it) still costs that layout again, which is the flow worth changing, not the fade.

The review's recheck (the fix-up, 2026-11-05, 20:10 to 20:20): `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening -Rounds 5`, nothing else of LBTerm's beside it (the lbterm-measure lock free, no build or check running), the PC locked (LogonUI), others taking 7 to 17% of the test's CPUs, every span calm. Before: the installed build, 20261105-130213-f9f79048 (main). After: the branch's tree at 31b2dcff with the kept menu light (committed as 0f86fd9b9), 20261105-200248-31b2dcff. `tab-menu-opening`: 2.2 against 1.41 ms, lower (+-0.53); p95 4.48 against 2.62, same (+-1.89); settled 2.2 against 255, higher (+-4.72); frames 1 against 2 and messages 3 against 4, higher; GUI thread 3.54 against 3.65 ms, same (+-17.3); CPU 5.32 against 4.68, same (+-23.9); private memory 402 and 402 MB, same. `prompts-opening`: 24 against 24.9 ms, same (+-3.63); settled 24 against 266, higher; frames 2 against 3 and messages 7 against 8, higher; GUI thread 10.2 against 24.1 ms, higher (+-3.83); CPU 13.1 against 28.1, higher (+-3.25); private memory 387 against 389 MB, higher (+-1.97). The medians passed their budgets in both builds, but round 5 of the branch's `tab-menu-opening` was over (GUI thread 22.4 ms against 10, CPU 30.5 against 20), and the half widths of +-17 and +-24 are why: the branch's runs are two kinds. Of its 75 measured runs, 50 took 2 to 6 ms of GUI thread, 5 took 8 to 40, and 20 took 40 to 172 ms (every round had some), each with the same 2 frames and 4 messages (2 WM_PAINT) and the process's CPU within a millisecond of its GUI thread's: work on the GUI thread, not a wait, and not more frames. Main's 75 runs were 2 to 6.0 ms, every one. Stage 6's 49.9 ms round was the same thing, so it isn't the fix-up's, and it isn't the PC: runs a few seconds apart on the same window differ by 150 ms with others taking under 5% of its CPUs. The recheck's suspects were the state file's frame after the fade and anything its paint makes again; the trace (next paragraph) found neither: the budgets stay 10 and 20 ms, which the runs without the spin meet.

Traced (2026-11-07, stage 2 of landing 2's follow-up; lbterm/FLOWS.md, "Opening, measured whole", has every step): the slow runs are the measuring's own. In each of the 25 runs over 8 ms, the round's `measure.json` names one task, present.rs:657, the timer of the test window's timing to the screen, run 1,784 to 57,686 times for 13 to 148 ms: `Present::arm` re-arms for a time already past while the fade's episode waits for DWM to compose a frame after its settle, a turn every 3.5 µs until DWM does or 200 ms pass (PITFALLS 164). It's main's code, off in Lachlan's window, and shows in test windows when DWM composes nothing for 30 ms after a motion settles, which on this PC means locked: every slow run was measured locked, and 51 runs unlocked (18:59, Lachlan at the PC) had none (GUI thread 4.7 to 7.5 ms, two runs of 4 frames at 15.6). A scan of the 1,212 rounds kept under `target/budgets` found 193 with a spinning run, main's `tab-by-key`, `zoom` and `throughput` rounds of 2026-11-05 among them (11 to 14 of 16 `tab-by-key` runs at 144 to 151 ms on f9f790489), so budgets set from locked rounds of actions with motion may hold it. Without it, the tab menu's opening costs 3.22 ms of GUI thread against main's 3.54. `prompts-opening`'s doubled GUI thread is something else, its runs without the spin 19.4 ms against 10.2: four paints of 21.5 ms against main's three of 8.1, the state file's frame after the fade among them.

Fixed (2026-11-07, stage 3 of landing 2's follow-up): `Present::arm` no longer arms a time already past (`Episode::next_look`, FLOWS.md "Opening, measured whole", step 6; PITFALLS 164). Measured once after, `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening -Rounds 5 -Bin <the branch's build> -Against <main's>`, 2026-11-07 19:47 to 19:59 in a warden GPU job with the lbterm-measure lock, the PC unlocked (Lachlan at it: Chrome's windows took three rounds' first tries, each taken again), others on 16 to 36% of the test's CPUs, other agents' test windows on the same screen at times (the harness moved the window clear of one). Before: main's build at this branch's merge base, 20261105-130213-f9f79048 (LBTerm-previous; no `.rs` file differs from f82fef807, the main merged in; the installed build had moved on to feat/context-pill's e8edad3fb, which this branch hasn't merged, so it would have measured that too). After: 624c78f6f's tree. Each cell main's then the branch's, Welch's verdict and its +-99% half width:

- `tab-menu-opening`: ms 1.98 against 1.51, lower (+-0.49); p95 4.52 against 2.64, same (+-3.74); GUI thread 3.75 against 3.70 ms, same (+-0.56); CPU 5.64 against 5.11 ms, lower (+-0.55); frames 1 against 2 and settled 1.98 against 256 ms, higher (the fade and the state file's frame after it); messages 3 against 6, higher (+-1.90; paired, same); private memory 666 against 669 MB, same (+-2.6). Run by run, the branch's GUI thread was 2.54 to 5.54 ms in all 90 of its runs (the five rounds and a round taken again), main's 3.06 to 8.10 in its 75; the timer's task (present.rs:654) ran at most 4 times in any of the branch's runs, main's (present.rs:657) 34 to 39 times, none of them a spin (main's menu plays no motion): looks at watched presents 20 ms apart, more of them on main's build for a reason not looked into.
- `prompts-opening`: ms 25.2 against 24.9, same (+-1.33); GUI thread 11.9 against 20.1 ms, higher (+-6.65); CPU 15.1 against 24.1, higher (+-9.25; paired, same); frames 2 against 3, higher; settled 25 against 262 ms, higher. Run by run 14.1 to 38.2 ms of GUI thread on the branch against 8.1 to 17.5 on main, with the timer's task at most 41 turns a run on either (the 20 ms looks at watched presents, no spin): what's left is the list's own, a paint more (the state file's frame after the fade) and dearer paints of the list's 40 rows, as the trace found, untouched here (FLOWS.md, "Opening, measured whole", What could go).

Unlocked, no run of either build spun before the fix either (FLOWS.md has the 51 unlocked runs of 18:59), so this run says the branch's openings are main's in every run, not that the spin has gone; that's the lbterm-extras tests' (`an_episode_whose_last_frame_never_comes_is_looked_at_a_refresh_apart_to_its_grace`), and a locked round's `measure.json`, whose present.rs:654 count a run should stay in the tens.

Every round of the branch's failed, and one of main's: `missed`, the actions' shared budget of 0, which only an episode walked with a present seen is judged on (the locked rounds of 2026-11-05 walked none). Every episode measured in this run, the branch's `menu.tab` and `menu.prompt` fades and main's one `outline.held` in its round 4, counted 1,383 to 8,285 refreshes against 9 to 14 presents in a fade of about 250 ms, and in some runs a first present before the episode opened (`motion_first_ms` -10.5): counts no screen makes, so the walk's `refreshes` weren't that screen's in these runs, on main's code as on the branch's (PITFALLS 166). Not traced: the motion line (`log::debug!` under `lbterm::present`) wasn't logged.

The branch as it lands (2026-11-07, 23:05 to 23:39, the last stage of landing 2's follow-up): `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening,inbox-opening,projects-opening,rail-opening -Bin <the branch's build> -Against <main's>`, 5 rounds, the openings this branch touches (the tab menu's facts and fade, the prompt list's and the InMail list's fade, the picker's kept light, the rail's hold under a card). After: 20261107-221735-1b8b60db, the branch's last code (the commits after it are docs). The review's rerun of `tab-menu-opening` (2026-11-08 01:38, 3 rounds, alone with the lock) was covered the same way. ### The report and the history

`report.json`: the run, the build (`version.txt`, else `lbterm --version`), the commit, when, the spans and their length, the summary (`pass`, `fail`, `error`), and each scenario as judged: `result`, `over` (each metric over, its value and its budget), `errors`, `bug`, `spans` (an action's runs, its warm-ups apart in `warmup`), `metrics` (each one's `unit`, `stat` (`median`, `p95` or `first`), `value` (what's judged; at rest `median` too), `max`, every span's or run's value, and with a budget `budget` (a most) or `budget_min` (a least, the throughput's), `why` and `pass`), `top` (who ran, added up over the spans or runs), `lines` (at rest what the GUI said ran at rest; for an action its own lines for it, each a run over the scenario's `gui_ms`), `notes`, and its folder. Each folder has the scenario's `measure.json` (every span or run as measured, an action's with its `top`, `wm_paint`, `tasks`, `state_ms` and `gui_count`, and every line the GUI logged on `lbterm::budget`; each sample's `top` has the five busiest sites of the requests, tasks, timers and threads, and every message's site and every PDU the window sent and took (`mux`), so a count two builds are told apart on by one message or a few PDUs can be traced to which: the review of perf/mux-client found zoom's 8 messages to 9 and conductor-pad's server CPU with nothing to say why; the scenario's `top` in report.json adds them up as `pdus_sent` and `pdus_received`), `output.txt`, `result.json`, the config and the state file.

`history.jsonl`: one line a run, `{run, date, build, commit, bin, runs, span_s, summary, scenarios: {<name>: {result, kind (an action's), <metric>: <value>, ...}}}`, appended, so one build's numbers against the next show a slow drift that no single run fails on. It's one file for every tree, in the main checkout's `target\budgets` (`Get-BudgetHistoryPath`, by git's common folder), whichever slot or worktree ran: until the review each tree kept its own, and nothing read them. Each run reads it first, and each scenario's medians are held against its last run's: one that moved by more than a quarter and more than 1 of its unit (a frame a second, a millisecond) is said under `drift` (the metric, what it was, what it is, the run it was in), in the table after the failures and counted in the summary's `drift`. Said, not judged: a budget is what fails a run, and a budget is never set from a build's own numbers where they're the bug.

Traced (2026-11-08, stage 2 of landing 2's third round; FLOWS.md, "A test window's motion, from an episode's opening to the budget's `missed`"; PITFALLS 166): the 19:47 run's `missed` was the walk's counting, on both builds. The walk took the screen's refreshes from its refresh counter, frame to frame, which add up to the counter at the last frame less the counter at the first present, and in every unlocked round of 2026-11-07 that kept motion figures (19:47 here, and perf/mux-client's at 20:13 on the installed main, e8edad3f, and its own build) the counter had run thousands ahead while the presents were as many as the motion's length a refresh: the tab menu's fade 1,392 to 7,689 refreshes against 8 to 14 presents in about 250 ms (the 60 Hz screen's 15 refreshes), in steps of 153 (2,156 and 2,003 the commonest); close-pane 7,695 to 16,757 against 30 to 32 in 560 ms; split 8,617 to 22,904 against 29 to 32; zoom 3,390 to 19,982 against 19 to 24 in 415 ms. Every run kept from before (42 runs of six scenarios, 2026-10-30 to 11-04) had counted 2 to 33. Every screen's counts read frame by frame showed the counter jumping at presents on three of the four screens while the vblank times stayed a refresh apart, so the walk counts by the times since then, and keeps the counter's as `counted` (`refreshes_counted` in each run's sample). Not measured since: the trace's runs (11:21 to 11:23, `tab-menu-opening` and `rail-opening`, 6 runs each on the branch's build with the trace and on a copy of the installed main) had the right-most screen covered by the DashboardsBox and Discord, 100%, so every episode was covered and `missed` went unjudged again; whether the menus' fades miss refreshes on a window that can be seen is still open. Main's build counts by the counter, so a `-Against` with it can't judge `missed` until this lands.

Measured on a window that could be seen (Lachlan's 8a, 2026-11-08 22:16 to 22:18 and 2026-11-09 00:09 to 00:11; DISPLAY1, the right-most screen, 0% covered by another program and no test window on it, which a watcher waited for between the other tracks' test jobs): `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening,inbox-opening` on a copy of the branch's build with his 3a and 4b (20261108-200000-0faee56b), 15 runs each, and `perf/budgets.ps1 -Scenario tab-menu-opening -Runs 6` with `lbterm::present::walk=trace`. Every run of every opening missed exactly 1 refresh (`missed` 1 in 45 of 45, of 13 or 14 `refreshes` by the vblank times; `refreshes_counted` 2,962 to 3,536, the counter still jumping), and the trace says where: the motion's first frame goes up (-7.6 to 11.5 ms from the episode's opening), its second refresh (11 to 29 ms in) comes without a present, and every refresh after it has one. The same window's rail motion did the same (`rail.row.grow` and the rest, 185 refreshes, 1 missed at 11 ms; `rail.light` and the rest, 94 refreshes, 1 missed at 20 ms), and so did each menu's fade out (7 or 8 refreshes, 1 missed at 13 to 29 ms). So it's the start of every motion this build plays, not the menus' fades: one refresh, about 17 ms on that 60 Hz screen, where the motion's first frame stays up a refresh longer. Main's build can't say whether it's new: the installed 63467391b, on the same runner, has no fade on the tab menu or the prompt list (`missed` unmeasured, the opening settled at its first frame, 1.55 and 23.4 ms), and its walk still counts by the counter (its rail's motion 30,680 refreshes and 30,507 missed in 2.9 s, the InMail list's 4,971 and 4,964). Why a motion's second refresh shows nothing new (DWM starting an animation a refresh after the commit that adds it, or LBTerm's first frame of it presenting a refresh late) isn't traced: LBTerm's Later has it.

A metric with a budget that nothing measured in any span or run (a build without it; `missed` or the screen's times for a window too hidden for anything to go up) isn't judged, as before, but no longer passes unsaid: the scenario's `unmeasured` names each, its entry in `metrics` says `unmeasured: true`, the table lists them under "budgets nothing measured (not judged)", and the summary counts the scenarios with any (`unmeasured`), so "40 within" says how many of the 40 were only partly checked. Until the review every action's `missed` (a budget of 0 on every action) counted toward "within" on a PC where no test window could be seen.

## What's hard to time

`lbterm-extras/src/present.rs` makes what Windows gives into what `metrics` reports under `present` (lbterm/CONTROL.md, "Metrics"), tested with the numbers handed in (`cargo test -p lbterm-extras present`); `wezterm-gui/src/present.rs` reads them from Windows, on the GUI thread. A frame hands over its visuals' swapchains as it presents them (`render/composited.rs`, one line, and a `chain` on the compositor's `Frame`), and its end says what it showed (termwindow/budget.rs's `lbterm_budget_frame_end`) and what plays (the compositor's in-flight list, `Compositor::playing`).

**Off until asked.** All of it runs only while it's on (`lbterm_extras::present::on`, one atomic load a frame): a test window turns it on with `LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING` in its environment (the budget runner's actions, `present.ps1`, `metrics.ps1 -Measure -Present`), and any LBTerm with `lbterm control metrics --present on` (and `off`). Off, a frame hands over no swapchain (no read lock on wgpu-hal's, no AddRef), nothing is watched, looked at or walked, no timer is armed and nothing is allocated for it, and what was under way is let go at the next frame; `metrics` says which in `present.on`. Until the review it ran in every window, Lachlan's included, while nobody had yet seen it work on a window in view (finding 39): a lock and an allocation or two each frame, the walk over what's above the window every 250 ms, and a 20 ms timer after each frame until its present was seen or 500 ms had gone. The runner's scenarios at rest leave it off, so what they measure is what his window spends.

### To the screen

Each action the budget times (Warnings in the daily build, "Expected times": a key's echo, a tab switch, a new tab, a split, the rail, the InMail list, the Conductor's view, a reload, and every frame's own paint) ends at a frame. Its first two times are to that frame's paint starting and to the end of its presents and commit, from what started it: for a key, the message's arrival in `wnd_proc` (slow.rs's `cause`, an Instant on QueryPerformanceCounter's clock). Both are said as the frame ends, whether or not anyone can see it.

The swapchain that shows it (a key's, its pane's visual; anything else's, the frame's last present) is noted with its present id (GetLastPresentCount) and read with GetFrameStatistics at the next frame's start, or by a timer of present.rs's own 20 ms on while nothing paints, until it says that present is up: its `SyncQPCTime`, a refresh's time on QueryPerformanceCounter's clock, put on the frame's own, is when it went up, and that less the action's start its third time. A present a later one overtook before it was read isn't timed (`overtaken`), nor one not up 500 ms after its present (`lost`), nor one whose window others hid (`hidden`, below). A frame that presented no visual, only a commit (the rail's opening, which DWM plays from what's drawn already), has its first two times and nothing to watch. A new swapchain's first answer is DXGI_ERROR_FRAME_STATISTICS_DISJOINT; one whose presents never went up answers zeros. Each swapchain is read once a look, however many presents are watched on it, and at most four presents are watched on one at a time, the oldest given up on as `lost`: a swapchain whose statistics never answer (a hidden window's) would otherwise keep each frame's watch for 500 ms, up to about 60 at 120 frames a second.

| `present.actions[]` | What it says |
|---|---|
| `count`, `seen` | its frames shown, and those seen going up |
| `hidden`, `overtaken`, `lost` | those not seen: its window hidden, a later present up first, not up within 500 ms |
| `paint_ms`, `present_ms` | from what started it to its frame's paint starting, and to its presents done: `mean` and `max` over `count` |
| `screen_ms`, `after_present_ms` | to the refresh it went up at, and from its presents to then (what DWM adds): over `seen` |
| `last` | the last one shown: its `paint_ms`, `present_ms`, and once seen `screen_ms` and `screen_unix_ms`, when it went up in Unix milliseconds, so a test times it from its own clock (budgets.ps1's `post_screen_ms`) |

### Frames missed during motion

LBTerm's motion is DWM's (lbterm/COMPOSITOR.md): the GUI hands it the curves and paints nothing while it plays, so a refresh that goes without a new frame is DWM or the GPU falling behind, not the GUI thread. An episode opens at the frame that starts motion (anything in the compositor's in-flight list still to end) and runs until the last of what plays settles, its names gathered as more start. As it opens, the GUI notes the compositor clock's last completed frame (DCompositionGetFrameId) and the counts DWM keeps for the window's screen as of it (DCompositionGetTargetStatistics' `presentedStats`: how many frames it has presented to that screen, the time of the screen's last refresh it saw, and the screen's refresh counter), the screen matched by its display adapter and source (D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromGdiDisplayName for the monitor's device name). Once it has settled (a timer 30 ms after; then, while the compositor hasn't completed a frame begun after the settle, again a refresh apart until it has or 200 ms have gone, `Episode::next_look`, never at a time already past, PITFALLS 164), and every 800 ms while it plays, so the compositor's history (its last 256 frames, 1.6 s at 160 Hz) doesn't run out under it, it walks the frames since, one per tick of the fastest screen's refresh: the first whose present count has moved is where the motion went up (`first_ms`, from the frame that started it), and from there to the last frame starting before it settles, the refreshes that came without a present are `missed`, the refreshes counted by the screen's vblank times (`presentedStats.time`, a refresh apart; `Walk::step`), not its refresh counter, which jumps at a present on this PC's screens (PITFALLS 166; until 2026-11-08 the walk took the counter's, and every `missed` judged on 2026-11-07 was its jump). The counter's count over the same span is kept beside it as `counted`, judged by nothing. The newest frame's counts aren't final until it's completed, and a frame's can come a frame late, so the misses are the whole span's refreshes less its presents, and `misses_ms` says where it fell short. Each paint the GUI made meanwhile is counted (`paints`), and those longer than a refresh of that screen (`slow_paints`): the content inside the moving visuals is late then, though the motion isn't.

The counts are every present DWM made to that screen, whoever's change it was: a refresh without one is one where nothing on that screen changed, and while LBTerm's motion plays there, something always has. What they do was seen on 2026-10-30 by sampling the newest completed frame at each compositor tick for 40 s, on the portrait screen beside the test windows' (other sessions' test windows were open there, in view): 73 presents in 83 refreshes over 1.5 s while something there moved, 0 to 3 in 30 otherwise, and on the test windows' own screen, all of it hidden, 0 to 8 in 30 whatever the test windows did. Read every compositor frame for 20 s on 2026-11-08 (`lbterm/perf/screen-counts.ps1`, no test window open; FLOWS.md, "A test window's motion, from an episode's opening to the budget's `missed`"): the present count moved by one a present and the time a refresh at a time on all four screens, while the refresh counter agreed with the time only on the main screen; on DISPLAY4 it went back 4 to 61 at 18 frames, each at or just after a present (393 refreshes by it, 1,200 by the times), on DISPLAY3 it lagged a refresh at each of 16 presents, and on DISPLAY1, the test windows' screen, it went back 39 at a present after a quiet spell. So refreshes are counted by the times.

| `present.motion` | What it says |
|---|---|
| `episodes`, `measured`, `covered`, `partial` | episodes settled; those walked with a present seen; those whose window was hidden as they opened, not walked; those the history ran out under, some frames passed over |
| `refreshes`, `presents`, `missed`, `with_misses` | over the measured ones, from each one's first present to its settling (refreshes by the screen's vblank times); the episodes with a miss |
| `counted` | the same span's refreshes by the screen's own refresh counter, which isn't a count on every screen (PITFALLS 166): kept to show when it and the times disagree, never judged; the budget runner's `refreshes_counted` |
| `first_ms` | from the frame that started an episode to its first frame up: `mean`, `max` |
| `paints`, `slow_paints`, `longest_paint_ms` | the GUI's paints while they played, those over a refresh, the longest |
| `names` | the same by each name that played (`pane.layout`, `rail.edge`, ...: lbterm/STATE.md's list), an episode under every name in it |
| `last` | the last one whole, `misses_ms` and its `lead` in refreshes included |

### Hidden windows

DWM presents a screen only when something on it changes, so a window others cover shows its frames and its motion to nobody, and its screen gets no frame for them: its present ids never go up (GetFrameStatistics answers zeros), and its motion would read as every refresh missed. So before either is trusted the GUI looks at what's over its window: the windows above it in the z-order (GetWindow up from it), visible, not minimized and not cloaked, by DWM's frame bounds, sampled at 16 by 16 points of the part of it on its monitor; 95% covered is hidden. It looks once an episode and at most every 250 ms for frames. A hidden window's actions are counted `hidden` and its episodes `covered`, never lost or missed. The runner's windows go where nothing covers them when their screen has such a place (the harness's `Move-TestWindowClear`: moved on its screen without being activated or raised, its size and place in the z-order kept), and `Get-WindowCover` says what's over one, by process and class, never title.

### Latency under load

Two runner scenarios: `key-echo-load` and `key-echo-load-local`, a key's echo at pwsh's prompt (as `key-echo`) with a writer in the pane beside it writing lines of 104 bytes 64 KB at a time without a pause, its title saying each second how much it wrote, which the console host takes as fast as LBTerm reads it (`writer_mb_per_s`). Their window never goes quiet, so a run waits 300 ms either side of the key instead, and its CPU and frames are the writer's too.

### What Windows can't give from inside, and what would

- **Before the message.** The time from the key going down to the message being taken: the keyboard's scan and its USB poll, the driver, the raw input thread, the queue. GetMessageTime says when Windows stamped the input, but in whole milliseconds of GetTickCount. The GUI's times start when it takes the message; the runner's `ms` and `post_screen_ms` start just before it posts the message, which leaves out the hardware and the raw input thread but takes in the queue. What would: a microcontroller that acts as a USB keyboard and watches the screen with a photodiode, timing a key to its light.
- **After the refresh.** `SyncQPCTime` is the vblank the frame went up at, the start of the screen's scan; the row with the echo is scanned out later in the refresh, and the panel then takes its own time to change. What would: the same photodiode, or a camera at 1,000 frames a second or more.
- **A hidden window.** Nothing it draws goes up, so there's nothing to time: hidden, not lost.
- **Someone else's word for it.** Everything here is DWM's word through DXGI and DirectComposition. PresentMon (Microsoft's, open source) traces every present through ETW (DxgKrnl's flip events and DWM's) and says when each went up, with nothing changed in LBTerm, but an ETW session needs an administrator or the Performance Log Users group, so an agent can't start it without elevation, and that asks in front of Lachlan. DXGI Desktop Duplication (IDXGIOutputDuplication) would see the screen's own updates with when each was presented (`LastPresentTime`) and where (dirty rectangles, no pixels needed), unelevated and without taking focus, but only what's on screen: it can see no more than the GUI does of a hidden window, so it wasn't built while the test windows' screen is covered (finding 39).
- **Windows 10.** The compositor clock's statistics are Windows 11's (dcomp.dll exports them from 22000): the GUI finds them by name, so an LBTerm on Windows 10 still starts, and says `compositor_clock: false`.
- **Without the compositor** (OpenGL, `lbterm_compositor = false`): no visual's swapchain is handed over, so its actions are timed to their paint and present only, and its motion is LBTerm's own frames, not DWM's, which this doesn't walk.

### What it costs

Off, nothing (above). On, every frame the compositor draws takes, for each visual it presents, its swapchain from wgpu (a read lock and an AddRef) and, for each action it shows, one GetLastPresentCount and a lock on the totals; then a GetFrameStatistics a swapchain at the next frame or the timer's 20 ms on, until its present is up. A look at what's over a window (at most every 250 ms while frames are watched, and once an episode) walks the windows above it: 0.27 to 0.32 ms in a test window with 423 windows above it (present.ps1's runs, 2026-10-30), 1.41 in the review's and 0.37 in the fix-up's, and close to nothing for a window at the top, as Lachlan's are while he works in them. Walking an episode is two calls into dcomp.dll a compositor frame (`cost.frames_walked`, `walk_ms`). Each is in the reply's `cost`. What a look at a swapchain's statistics and a walk cost couldn't be measured here: with every test window hidden, nothing was watched or walked (finding 39).

The whole of it, as GUI CPU with this stage's measuring and without (`perf/metrics.ps1 -Measure`: 400 keys typed into a pwsh prompt 8 ms apart, then a pane printing as fast as pwsh writes for 8 s, about 41 MB, in metrics.ps1's own test window, WebGpu with the compositor, max_fps 120). Before is the installed build (20261030-191144-301f18e6, main as Lachlan runs it, with none of this branch: no counters, no budget, no `present`); after is this branch's (53180a56 with 5f5bee9c0's code). Runs interleaved, before then after, local then mux, 2026-10-30 20:14 to 20:19, Lachlan at the PC, no cargo or rustc running and two or three `lbterm-gui` at each start. The window was hidden under the Dashboards box, as every test window was, so the after build said its actions' times and looked at what covered it, and watched and walked nothing: what a window in view spends on its swapchains' statistics isn't in these. CPU in ms, the median and every run (Windows' CPU times, in 15.6 ms ticks):

| | GUI thread before | GUI thread after | GUI process before | GUI process after | frames before / after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| typing, local (3 runs each) | 1000 (1016, 1000, 984) | 1000 (1281, 1000, 969) | 2547 | 2750 | 381 / 377 |
| output, local | 2516 (2578, 2516, 2406) | 2531 (2547, 2531, 2438) | 12984 | 13188 | 482 / 482 |
| typing, mux domain | 953 (953, 1078, 813) | 984 (953, 1078, 984) | 2125 | 2266 | 381 / 380 |
| output, mux domain | 4641 (4438, 4938, 4641) | 4797 (4281, 5000, 4797) | 9266 | 9859 | 480 / 478 |

Every difference is inside a build's own spread, and these take in the first four stages' counters and budget as well as this one's.

### What it measured

On 2026-10-30, on this branch's build (20261030-193134-53180a56, with 5f5bee9c0's code), Lachlan at the PC, every test window hidden under the Dashboards box on its screen (finding 39): at every scenario's start the harness found the box over 100% of the window's part on screen, and often another session's test window too. So the GUI timed every action to its paint and its presents, said every frame it watched as hidden and every episode of motion as covered, and judged none of them on the screen or on missed refreshes: there was nothing on screen to judge.

The whole suite, `lbterm.ps1 budgets`, from 19:48:54 to 20:12:53, no cargo or rustc running at its start and two other `lbterm-gui` (Lachlan's daily one and one other test's): 40 within their budgets, 7 over, none that couldn't be measured, in 24 minutes, 2 scenarios passing on their second try (`rail-pad`, whose mux server hadn't answered, and `copy-mode`). The seven over are the seven known: at rest `cursor-blink` (6.87 ms a second of the GUI thread against 5, 4.21 tasks a second against 4, findings 14 and 8), `conductor-view` (4.11 frames a second, 24.6 ms a second of the GUI thread, finding 12) and `tasks-card` (one frame a second, 4.9 ms, finding 15); of the actions `key-echo` and `key-echo-local` (22.3 and 21.8 ms against 20, finding 35), `start` (its pane at 2,746 ms against 1,900, finding 25) and `throughput` (2.71 MB a second against at least 4.8, finding 24). The three new actions passed: `close-pane` 27.9 ms (p95 32), `key-echo-load` 31.4 ms (p95 51.1), `key-echo-load-local` 56.1 ms (p95 74.2). Every other scenario at rest and every other action came out as in the rounds before (the tables above): no frames at rest, 0.05 to 0.15 ms a second of the GUI thread.

What each action's own times said, the median of its 15 runs (8 for a reload), from its message's arrival:

| Action | to its frame's paint | to its presents done (the GUI's own) | from the post, to the frame (the runner's) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `key-echo` | 17.7 | 22.3 | 22.3 |
| `key-echo-local` | 17.5 | 21.9 | 21.8 |
| `key-echo-load` | 24.9 | 30.9 | 31.4 |
| `key-echo-load-local` | 23.9 | 47.3 | 56.1 |
| `tab-by-key` | 2.0 | 6.7 | 6.6 |
| `tab-by-click` | 2.5 | 7.3 | 7.1 |
| `new-tab` | 23.8 | 34.2 | 34.0 |
| `new-tab-local` | 54.1 | 60.5 | 60.7 |
| `close-tab` | 4.2 | 10.8 | 10.6 |
