68. **On a mux domain the server's answer to every key painted a frame that drew nothing.** The server sends a pane's changes back at once for each key (`SendKeyDown` in wezterm-mux-server-impl's sessionhandler.rs, for local echo's cursor), before the program has echoed it, and the client notified `PaneOutput` for every delta, changed or not. With the key's own frame gone that answer's frame came first, ending 4.7 to 9.8 ms after the key, and held the echo's a pacing period: key-echo's two frames a key were both asked for by `mux_pane_output_event`, the echo at 21.8 to 28.8 ms. *Fixed by perf/key-latency:* `apply_changes_to_surface` (wezterm-client's renderable.rs) notifies only for a delta that changes something: the server's seqno, a dirty line, the cursor, the size, the title, the folder, a line the cache doesn't hold yet, the mouse grab or the alternate screen, and every delta while local echo is on, as upstream did. One that changes nothing is said on `lbterm::echo` at debug; `perf/echo-frame.ps1 -Mux` saw 8 for 8 keys. The server still sends each answer: not sending one that changes nothing would be the server's own change (perf/mux-server's ground).
69. **The scroll strip's prompt marks worked out every new row's semantic zones with the pane's terminal held.** `lbterm_prompt_marks` (`render/marks.rs`, the strip's and the scrollbar's) looks at each frame at every row of the scrollback that changed since its last look, for an OSC 133 prompt in it, through `Line::semantic_zone_ranges`, which works out and keeps the row's zones: two passes over its cells, an attribute compared for each, and for a row the scrollback keeps as runs of attributes (`ClusteredLine`, every row once it scrolls off the screen) each cell made from its run with the text's graphemes split again. About 4.5 us a row, all of it inside the pane's `with_lines_mut`: with a pane printing 16 MB at full speed (about 2,600 new rows a frame) 11.7 ms of every frame's 26 to 28, the terminal held throughout and the parser waiting on it (finding 37). Its cost went with the rows printed, not the frames, so `max_fps` 10 showed output only 3% faster. *Fixed by perf/key-latency:* a row holds a prompt when any run of its attributes is a prompt's (`Line::has_semantic_type`, read off a clustered row's runs, `ClusteredLine::any_attrs`), which needs no zones worked out: 0.6 ms a frame, 0.25 us a row (`perf/output-hold.ps1`). The look through 50,000 rows of scrollback for the rows that changed (`get_changed_since`) was left; it was within the 0.6 ms. Main's perf/lbterm-layers changed the marks beside it (finding 65: rows read only as they're written, a long scrollback a chunk at a time between frames, a mux pane's asked of its server), with a first look at a row's cells before its zones (`mux/src/lbterm_prompts.rs`, `is_prompt`); merging it, that first look is `Line::has_semantic_type`'s, so the GUI's own panes and the server's answers to `GetPromptRows` both read a scrollback row off its runs.
70. **A frame reads each tab's user vars, titles and program status with their panes' terminals, which a printing pane's parser holds three quarters of the time.** The rail's rows (`panel_tab`), the strip and the state file ask a local pane for `copy_user_vars`, `get_title`, `get_program_status`, its cursor and its size, each taking its terminal's lock, and a pane printing at full speed has its parser applying a batch 7.1 ms of every 9.7. With finding 37 fixed the rail's reading of its tabs waited 3.8 ms a frame at the median (5.3 ms of the rail's paint, 1.5 of it CPU) and a frame's copy of the rows 7.6 ms at the 95th percentile (0 at the median): time the GUI thread waits with nothing to do, keys included (finding 44), though it no longer costs output's speed. And the implicit hyperlink rules (`apply_hyperlinks`, Lachlan's adds two to the defaults) scan every new row on the screen with the terminal held, 1.3 ms a frame while output streams. Not looked into: the parser applying its batch in smaller pieces, or what the rail reads kept beside the terminal, would each shorten the waits; both are the mux's own files (perf/mux-server's ground). And a frame's copy compares each row it keeps from the last with the terminal's, cell by cell, about 8 us a row of 160 cells each with its 24-bit colour on the heap (0.5 ms a frame for a screen of them): a row whose sequence number hasn't moved hasn't changed, but trusting that alone needs every way a row changes to move it, which nothing holds the terminal to.

Found by the review of perf/key-latency on main's build (71).

71. **A test's update-status handler that only wrote a file wasn't called again on its interval** (written first as "after a reconnect the window's Lua doesn't see the server's panes in time"). `perf/mux-reconnect.ps1` has its test server hang up on the GUI (`LBTERM_TEST_HANGUP`) and reads the panes the window's Lua reports from its `update-status` handler. The reconnect was right all along: `Reconnected!` in the GUI's log about a second later, the window still open, the server's `list-clients` naming it again, and a tab the server makes after it reaching the window. But `update-status` comes again on `status_update_interval` only after the handler calls `window:set_right_status` or `set_left_status`, upstream's rule too: `schedule_next_status_update` is armed at the end of a title update, by those two and by a modifier key, never by the event itself. The test's handler only wrote its report, so after the reconnect it ran only if some title update happened along. Main's build, whose process walks raised title alerts now and then, passed 1 run of 3 (2026-11-01, unlocked, interleaved); perf/key-latency's, which walks a pane's processes only when they change, failed 4 of 4; and the review saw both fail on 2026-10-31. Fixed in the test: the handler sets an empty right status every time. With that, interleaved on 2026-11-01, perf/key-latency's build passed 3 runs of 3 and main's (7f67863f6) 3 of 3. Lachlan's config has no `update-status` handler in daily use, so nothing of his waited on it. PITFALLS.md, trap 48.

What perf/fonts-core found (72 to 93; numbered after main's, which landed first: 72 to 91 were 56 to 75 on the branch): font setup 72 to 80, printing 81 to 85, upstream's metrics and the caches 86 to 90, a busy pane 91, and from the whole suite's rounds 92 and 93.

72. **Every config save made every font again, through the system's font lookup** (fonts-3). 11 system font files copied out of GDI and parsed a save, 14 at a start, all of them substitutes for families Windows doesn't have and turned down by name, then every glyph rasterized again into a new atlas and every line shaped again. With twenty lines of Claude Code's symbols on screen a reload took 227 to 312 ms to the frame and 263 to 350 ms of the GUI thread. *Fixed here*: 74 to 109 ms and 72 to 105 ("What font setup costs").
73. **Each font searched for its own fallbacks, and each find reshaped the window twice** (fonts-6): once for the search's completion, once as the next shape with that font threw the paint pass away part-way. A line in a new script, in a pane and its tab's title: 102 to 110 ms to the frame, 121 to 135 ms of the GUI thread. A reload or a zoom searched again for everything on screen. *Fixed here*: one search, two reshapes, no pass thrown away, 59 to 70 ms and 92 to 105; and a reload, a zoom or a new window searches for nothing found before.
74. **A window's first atlas was 128 px a side, cleared before it grew** (fonts-5). A first screen of 500 characters in three styles made three atlases and cost the GUI thread 307 to 331 ms; from 1024, none and 238 to 272. *Fixed here.* A full atlas is still made again whole, every shape with it; how often that happens in a long-running window is now counted (`fonts.atlas_cleared`) and not yet known.
75. **The window's caption font was read from its whole file on every reload**: `apply_theme`'s `update_title_font` on `config_did_change`, through GDI and FreeType. *Fixed here*: kept while its LOGFONT and dpi are the same.
76. **Choosing the GPU adapter is the longest step of a window's start**: wgpu makes a D3D12 device on every adapter DXGI lists to ask what each can do, 308 to 524 ms. It ran on the GUI thread for every window. *Here* it runs once a process, on a thread of its own from the config's end; the first frame comes no sooner when that thread is slowed beside the GUI thread's own start, as in three of five rounds ("A window's start, step by step").
77. **Every device compiled two compute pipelines LBTerm never uses**: wgpu's checks of indirect draws and dispatches, on in its release builds (`InstanceFlags::VALIDATION_INDIRECT_CALL`), 30 to 40 ms a device. *Fixed here.*
78. **A window's first frame makes 27 compositor surfaces, 3 to 15 ms each whatever their size**: 126 to 169 ms of a first draw of 118 to 198 ms. Each `wgpu::Surface::configure` waits for the device to be idle and makes a composition swapchain; a 1 by 1 surface takes 3.2 ms. Open: the compositor's.
79. **A zoom's remaining cost is the compositor's surfaces made again at the window's new size**: of 78 to 109 ms of the GUI thread a step in a plain window, the draw 75 to 77 ms; its fonts 8 ms. Open: the compositor's, as finding 78.
80. **The measuring's own bug, caught before it landed: a test window's console prints in its code page.** A writer that wrote UTF-8 bytes straight to its standard output showed other characters, so `budgets.ps1`'s fallback line was mojibake in JetBrains Mono's own range until it set `[Console]::OutputEncoding`; its first two rounds measured nothing of fallbacks and aren't counted.

81. **A 24-bit colour or a link was a heap block a cell, made for every character printed and every copy of a line** (core-3). A pen with one copied for each character: a line of 100 printed with 104 allocations against a palette pen's 3, a copy of a line held a cell at a time 101; Claude Code's look performed at 72 ns a byte with 492,331 allocations a MB, and on a mux domain showed 16 MB in 26 to 27 s for 30 s of the server's CPU and 16 of the GUI thread's. *Fixed here* (shared fat attributes, the pen by reference): 15.7 ns a byte, 33,266 allocations a MB, 12.0 to 13.2 s, 7.0 to 7.6 s of the server's and 3.3 to 3.6 of the GUI thread's ("What printing costs").
82. **Printing went a grapheme at a time, and the parser a byte at a time** (core-12, which the audit called plausible). Plain lines performed at 53 ns a byte and parsed at 21; 16 MB showed locally in 2.1 s. *Fixed here* (a run of ASCII at once, the parser's runs, its strings taken whole): 6.3 and 3.0 ns a byte, 0.7 to 0.8 s, 18.8 to 21.9 MB/s; a mux domain's server spends 2.0 to 2.1 s on it against 3.4. Its smaller parts measured and left: TermConfig's two locks a lookup (under 1% of the parser thread), vtparse's 2 KB array an escape dispatch zeroes (none in the output measured).
83. **A line scanned for links was left a cell at a time** (core-8): a copy of a scanned line of Claude Code's look 21 allocations and 2,527 bytes. *Fixed here*: 2 and 318. Open: the scan itself, 33 to 40 µs a line with Lachlan's eight rules, the five with Unicode word boundaries 4 to 10 µs each in the regex engine, on every line a mux client receives and every changed line a local pane draws (links per line, the LBTerm layers' track).
84. **The line caches held quads 2.6 times upstream's size**, what made 100,000 lines of output cost about 20 MB more than in upstream WezTerm (lbterm/BENCH.md): LBTerm's corners and gradient inline in every `BoxedQuad`. *Fixed here*, and on main by feat/switch-calls the same way (two boxes, 104 bytes a quad, where this branch's one made 96; the merge keeps main's): a window like the benchmark's grows 37.9 to 39.3 MB over 800,000 lines where the installed build grew 70.4 to 72.7 and upstream 35 over 100,000. perf/line-quads-by-content (2026-11-04) keeps a line's quads as the four vertices each is copied in as and its hash, 280 bytes a quad, so the line quad cache can be copied into a layer in one block and moved to its row (lbterm/FLOWS.md rows 85 to 93): the line quad cache's part of this growth comes back: full, a window grows about 1.6 times what main's does (finding 97), Lachlan's call against the speed (his rule leans to speed). Kept as the 84 bytes of fields a quad is drawn from, inline with no box, and turned into vertices as they're copied in, it would hold what this fix holds and still need no allocation a quad and no setter a vertex.
85. **Locally the GUI paints a pane under its lock, and the parser waits on it a third of its time.** Wide characters and emoji show at 4.9 MB/s locally (4.7 before this stage) where the model prints them at 18.5: sampled, the parser thread spends 31% of its time parked on the pane's lock, the GUI thread 29 to 32%, and the GUI's paint under the lock goes mostly on LBTerm's own layers, the shell scroll strip's prompt search (25 to 35% of the GUI thread's samples, every line's zones computed), the triggers (14 to 17%) and the rail's titles (9 to 13%), then drawing the lines. Open: those are the LBTerm layers' track; drawing a local pane from a copy of its lines, as a mux client's are, would let the parser run while the GUI draws.

86. **The stats thread woke every second at rest** (fonts-7, paint-12) to read `periodic_stat_logging` and find it off. *Fixed by main's perf/at-rest* (lbterm/AT-REST.md), before this stage.
87. **Every record of upstream's metrics took the recorder's global lock and a SipHash lookup** (fonts-7, paint-12, events-10): 369 a frame of two panes of still lines, more for each line laid out, each main-thread task, each PDU and each read of a local pane's output on its parser thread, 105 ns a record. *Fixed here*: each thread's own handles, 55 ns; the caches' hits and misses counted with a relaxed add each. A frame of cached lines costs the GUI thread about 0.1 ms less (1 to 2%); `throughput-local` 8% less of it ("What upstream's metrics cost").
88. **The LFU caches put out the entries they had just put in** (fonts-8, plausible), in a cache whose every entry had been looked up many times lately: five rounds of misses in lfucache's test. Not seen in a window's caches, whose decay brings lines seen less often to nothing first. *Fixed here* all the same: an entry just used is kept, and of the least frequently used the least recently used goes first ("What the caches keep").
89. **A frame with nothing new in it costs the GUI thread 5 to 7.5 ms.** Two panes of still text in Lachlan's look: the budget runner's `repaint` 4.9 to 5.1 ms of the GUI thread and 4.6 to 4.7 ms of `gui.paint.impl`, of which drawing the panes' 122 lines (`paint_pane.lines`) 1.0 to 1.1 ms and, of that, copying their cached quads in (`quad_buffer_apply`) 0.54 to 0.62; painted 50 times a second (`line-cache.ps1 -Measure`), 7.3 to 7.8 ms a frame, about 37% of a core. Where the other 3.5 ms of a frame go wasn't looked into here: the rail, the strips and the compositor's draw and present are the paint track's, and a line found in the cache still costs 8 to 20 µs of `paint_pane.lines`.
90. **A tab's lines are laid out again once more lines were shown since than the caches hold**: with 1,024 entries, 18 tabs of 61 lines visited in turn laid out every line of every revisit, before this stage's change to the eviction and after. *Fixed by main's feat/switch-calls*, which made the line caches 4,096 entries by default (with 20 tabs of 90 rows a side-button hop lays nothing out again; the README's line on it).
91. **A pane printing at full speed on a mux domain held up every other pane and the cli** (this branch, until perf/mux-server was merged in). The printing stage's faster parser brought it out: the budget runner's `key-echo-load` errored on this branch's builds from c6283f833 on, the echo beside a full-speed writer never showing (in 15 s, and in a probe in 120 s), where the installed build echoes in 35 to 52 ms; `key-echo-load-local` passed. Sampled, the server's parser thread performed under the pane's lock in 86 to 94% of its samples and parking_lot's plain unlock let it take the lock straight back, so the server's main thread waited on it 93% of its time, and every output notification queued a push of the pane's changes of its own on the main thread, faster than they ran: `lbterm cli get-text` of the other pane went unanswered for minutes. A fair unlock (`MutexGuard::unlock_fair`) and one push queued at a time for a client and a pane got get-text answered in 101 to 167 ms, but the key still didn't echo: in 10 s the window sent 2,093 to 2,152 `GetLines` for the busy pane and was answered 0 to 3 times, its `SendKeyDown` behind them, and neither the server's loop taking reads and queued writes in turn nor keeping the socket's readiness between its turns (each tried in a build of its own) changed that. *Fixed by perf/mux-server* (lbterm/MUX.md, "A window that can't keep up holds the server to what it takes"), merged in on 2026-10-31: its connection owes each client one push a pane, worked out when the client can take it, and the window takes the pushes of the pane it typed into first. This branch's push queued at a time gave way to it, and the fair unlock, measured no longer needed, went too (`busy-pane.ps1`, two rounds interleaved on the merged build: five echoes each 31 to 90 ms and get-text 102 and 81 ms without it, 31 to 115 ms and 103 and 92 ms with it). `perf/busy-pane.ps1` holds it, on a mux domain and (-Local) in the window's own tab.
92. **A new tab by F8, or its close by F6, now and then never showed, on main's build as on this branch's.** In the whole suite's rounds of 2026-10-31 (13:20 to 15:48, Lachlan at the PC: Chrome windows of his came up just after his own input) this branch's first round errored in `new-tab` and `new-tab-local`, each twice, waiting 20 s for the tab F6 closes or 15 s for the frame with the tab F8 opens. The installed build's second round lost the same kind of key the same way: `split`'s close by F3 and `close-pane`'s pane, each waited on for 20 s. Run 40 times a round to see whose it was (15:48 to 15:52, Lachlan at the PC again), `new-tab-local` timed out waiting for the new tab's frame once on each build: after 7 runs on the installed build (20261030-191144-301f18e6), and after 15 in this branch's second round, its first passing all 40; the PC went down 10 runs into the installed build's second. Run again on 2026-11-01 (18:21 to 18:27, unlocked, other sessions' test windows and a build beside it), 40 runs a round, two rounds of each interleaved, on the merged build (stamped 9af4029e) and the installed one (main's 7f67863f): 160 runs, none timed out, the merged build's new tab in 69.5 and 66.1 ms at the median (p95 108 and 85.5, the GUI thread 58.6 and 58.7 ms) and the installed build's in 84.5 and 78.5 (p95 112 and 116, 87.2 and 85). So it isn't this branch's, nor the locked desktop's; what loses it, rarely, isn't known: the next time it fails, the window's log and its key counts (`metrics`' `messages`) say whether F8 or F6 arrived.
93. **On a mux domain a key's echo beside a full-speed writer has a longer tail with this branch, its writer printing four times as much.** In the four runs of the merged build (the whole suite's two and the two against main's, 2026-11-01) `key-echo-load`'s p95 was 51, 184, 174 and 145 ms where the installed build's and main's four were 27 to 123 (one over its budget of 90); the median 24.5 to 28 against 18.9 to 27.3. The writer beside the key took 46 to 51 MB a second against 11.8 to 12.7, its output parsed four times as fast (finding 82), and the GUI thread spent 324 to 337 ms over the run against 170 to 239: the window draws four times the output the key's echo waits behind, and in the slowest runs three of fifteen echoes took 95 to 184 ms. In the window's own tab the same scenario is better (`key-echo-load-local` 41 and 56 ms at the median against the installed build's 62 and 84). Open: a key's echo isn't put ahead of a busy pane's pulled lines on a mux domain (finding 24, and perf/mux-client's track); budgeting the echo against a writer held to main's rate would tell the two apart.
94. **Main's own local output is half what its budget and its README line say** (main's; PITFALLS 46). `throughput-local`, 16 MB of plain lines in a window's own tab, on main's builds with the PC unlocked on 2026-11-01: main at 6cc0bc7ef (perf/key-latency landed) 2,409 and 1,858 ms (6.52 and 8.45 MB/s; Against main's own build, above), and the installed build of 66785005 (feat/tab-connections) 2,347 and 2,343 ms (6.69 and 6.7 MB/s, 92 frames; the review of this branch, 22:28 to 22:30), against its budget of at least 11.5 MB/s and the README's line of about 1.3 s. perf/key-latency measured that on its own branch, unlocked too, with perf/mux-server and then feat/switch-calls merged in (1,264 to 1,313 ms, 11.85 to 12.37 MB/s; Output, after perf/key-latency, b08366771), so something that came into main after it seems to have taken back about half. Not this branch's: its printing changes take the parser off the limit, and it shows the 16 MB in 365 to 416 ms beside those same main builds. On 2026-11-03 main's build of de9558132 showed it at 10.95 to 11.53 MB/s (1,362 to 1,433 ms; five rounds against this branch, Against main's build of 2026-11-03, above), about its budget and its README line again, so whatever took half of it from 6cc0bc7ef and 66785005 isn't in today's main, or was those rounds' conditions: both ran beside other tracks' test windows, before the budget runner watched the PC. Not bisected: a build of each merge between would say which, if it matters.

Found by perf/gpu-memory, from the first two items of the groundwork's memory report (`lbterm/bench/mem/README.md`, "What could go without costing speed") (95 and 96).

95. **Each window's GPU allocator committed 256 MB of the GPU's memory and 64 MB of RAM for the few MB a terminal draws with.** `request_device` in `termwindow/webgpu.rs` took wgpu's default `MemoryHints::Performance`, whose D3D12 allocator (gpu-allocator, through wgpu-hal's `dx12/suballocation.rs`) takes blocks of 256 MB in the GPU's memory and upload blocks of 64 MB in RAM, and each window makes a device of its own: in a window drawn as Lachlan's at rest with one tab, a private allocation of 256 MB committed and never resident and one of 64 MB resident and nearly all zeros. *Fixed here*: blocks of 128 MB and 64 MB (`config::lbterm::GPU_MEMORY_BLOCK`), about 129 MB less private memory a window and 128 MB less of the GPU's ("What a window's GPU memory holds"; 64 MB and 32 MB blocks saved 225 MB, 192 MB and 32 MB of RAM, but cost speed, below). MemoryUsage's 8 MB and 4 MB blocks would save about 170 MB, 112 MB and 60 MB more, but made the atlas filling again a quarter slower: each glyph it takes is staged in an upload buffer of its own, 64 KB at least, so a refill made and let go dozens of 4 MB blocks. And held to a test (`atlas-refill`, paired over 16 rounds), the 64 MB blocks' 32 MB upload blocks put a screen of glyphs the atlas doesn't hold 5.5% later (28 ms of 521) than the default's, where 128 MB blocks (64 MB upload blocks) cost nothing measured and save 155 MB a window against the defaults, 97 MB less than 64 MB's, so the blocks are 128 MB under Lachlan's rule in LBTerm, speed over memory ("A screen of glyphs it hadn't drawn, held to a test").
96. **Each window's D3D12 view heap held 1,000,000 views, 30.5 MB of the GPU's own memory, for the few thousand bind groups a window keeps at most.** wgpu-hal's D3D12 backend makes the shader-visible view heap whole with the device, as many views as the device's `max_non_sampler_bindings`, and `request_device` in `termwindow/webgpu.rs` asked for `Limits::downlevel_defaults()`, which carry wgpu's 1,000,000: 32 bytes a view, mapped into the process from the GPU's memory, all zeros, in private bytes but never the working set (the groundwork's report had taken it for RAM). A window keeps the atlas's two bind groups and a uniform's for each place a visual is drawn at, 18 at rest with four panes; it kept 1,703 after 150 sizes of a resize, a buffer of 64 KB of the GPU's memory each. *Fixed here*: 65,536 views (`config::lbterm::GPU_VIEW_DESCRIPTORS`), 2 MB, and the uniforms kept to the window's size and the one before and to a quarter of a block (`GPU_UNIFORMS_KEPT`, 512 and 32 MB with the 128 MB blocks, 256 and 16 MB with the 64 MB blocks these were measured with), which only the frame count held before: 28.4 MB less private memory a window and 28.7 MB less of the GPU's, the working set the same; four windows of one GUI 781 MB where they were 887; and a window resized 600 times holds 26 bind groups and 170 MB of GPU memory, where it held 5,570 and 490 ("What a window's GPU memory holds", "A window resized"). With finding 95, held against the installed build by the budget runner, 22 scenarios: nothing in speed told apart once run again, and nothing at all against the branch's own build without the two changes (spinner-tall and throughput-local, ten rounds each); four windows 1,782 MB to 773.

Found by perf/line-quads-by-content, measured once against main's build (03550aa2, main's code on 2026-11-04) after the review (97).

97. **A pane's line was laid out again wherever it moved, and every line on screen was built again from boxed fields every frame.** The line quad cache's key held the row's pixel y and index, so a scroll or Claude Code moving its conversation up a region laid every moved row out again, and a kept line's quads were `Box<BoxedQuad>`s whose four vertices each were built again through the setters as they were copied into the layer (FLOWS.md rows 85 to 87, 91 and 93; What could go, 3). *Fixed here*: the key is what the line shows (`render/line_key.rs`), a line is laid out at row 0 and kept as flat vertices with their hashes, and it's copied in as one block moved to its row with the row folded into each hash. `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only busy-scroll,busy-lines,busy-one-tab,key-echo,one-tab -Against <main's build> -Rounds 5`, 2026-11-04 03:52 to 04:12 in a warden GPU job, the PC locked throughout (LogonUI running), others on 2 to 14% of the test's CPUs, no round busy, main's first in each cell: `busy-scroll` (Claude Code's fullscreen view, every row drawn again a line on every 50 ms) the GUI thread 41.2 and 31.3 ms a second (lower, +-6.18), the pass 2.42 and 1.13 ms (lower, +-0.573), lines laid out 102 and 2.99 a second (lower, +-24.9), the window's private memory 419 and 381 MB (lower, +-8.48), the frame 6.21 and 5.15 ms and the process's CPU 66.7 and 59.7 ms a second not told apart (+-1.16, +-9.35); `busy-lines` (a ticker under 26 lines, every row but the ticker a hit on both builds, so what differs is the copy) the pass 1.33 and 0.898 ms (lower, +-0.121), the frame 2.84 and 2.4 (lower, +-0.32), frames 5.98 and 6.36 a second and private memory 365 and 368 MB (higher, past +-0.212 and +-2.15), the GUI thread 18.5 and 15 ms a second not told apart (+-3.12); `busy-one-tab`, `one-tab` and `key-echo` the same in every metric. The copy's part, about 0.44 ms of a 27-row frame, is about 16 µs a row, what proposal 3 worked out from run 4 (14). A full line quad cache holds more, 280 bytes a quad against about 130 (perf/gui-thread.ps1's fill, two rounds of each build interleaved, 04:27 to 04:31): 20,000 varied lines grew a window of 90 rows by 105 and 106 MB on main and 167 and 170 here, lines the pane's width in runs of colour by 205 and 206 against 333 and 332, the scrollback in each alike, so about 63 and 127 MB more a window once its caches fill; the fill laid out 4,484 to 4,890 lines against main's 6,195 to 6,203 (-FillWide 14,469 to 14,649 against 17,564 to 17,743) and took the GUI thread 1,184 to 1,448 ms against 1,228 to 1,611 (-FillWide 12,471 to 12,927 against 13,717 to 13,765), two rounds each, not tested. The form that keeps a quad's 84 bytes of fields inline (finding 84) would hold what main holds, at a cost to the copy not measured: Lachlan's call. With the PC locked a window drew about 6.5 frames a second of `busy-scroll`'s 20 (the whole suite's rounds of 2026-11-01, the PC unlocked, drew 20 to 20.3, and `budgets.json`'s `lines` budget counts 583 to 604 lines laid out a second, every row each frame), so each frame took in about three scrolls: the per-frame figures (the pass) hold, while the per-second ones are a locked desktop's, and unlocked main would lay out about 29 rows a frame where this lays out about one. perf/line-quads.ps1 holds that a scroll lays out only the line it brings and that a lone line moved keeps no quad stale; its captures weren't compared, the PC being locked.

98. **perf/present.ps1 passed only while its window was covered, and loses a quarter to three quarters of its keys when it isn't** (2026-11-09, Lachlan's 2b). Its passes (2026-11-07 21:49 to 2026-11-08 16:22) were every one a window 100% covered on the far-right screen (Lachlan's Dashboards box, Discord, other tests' windows: "nowhere clear on its screen"), so all 12 keys counted `hidden`, nothing was timed to the screen, and the script passed on its paints and presents alone. From 2026-11-08 19:37 the screen was clear ("over the window: nothing"), the keys were watched, and every build lost some: 1 to 9 of 12 a run (`lost`, not up 500 ms after their present, `LOST_MS`), on main's builds as on branches', and on the build installed before the first pass (63467391, run 2026-11-09 22:13: 6 to 8 of 12). So the change was the window being seen, not the PC or the code: no driver, Windows or display-setting change that evening, and lbterm.lua's one change (17:50) acts only on a click. Every failing run's `available` says `frame_statistics: true` with `frame_statistics_error` 0x887A000B (DISJOINT) seen at least once, while the compositor clock walked every motion episode of the same window (its refreshes and presents counted), so DWM was composing that screen. Why GetFrameStatistics doesn't reach a key's present within 500 ms isn't traced: whether it answers DISJOINT for the whole of it or counts that stay behind the watched present. `lbterm::present::stats` at trace (WEZTERM_LOG) logs every read, its present, its age and what the read said, to tell which; the run with it (2026-11-09 22:27) found the window 100% covered by the Dashboards box again and read nothing. With the screen clear (Lachlan moved the box, his 2a; 2026-11-09 23:34, the build of feat/look-tests' tip, `lbterm.ps1 e2e -Only present -TestLog 'info,lbterm::present::stats=trace,lbterm::present=debug'`), "over the window: nothing", every one of the 581 reads of five swapchains' statistics in present's window answered: 10 DISJOINT (0x887A000B), each the first read after a present, and 571 success with `PresentCount`, `PresentRefreshCount` and `SyncRefreshCount` all 0, from 14 ms to past 500 ms after the present. So the statistics of a DirectComposition visual's swapchain (wgpu-hal's D3D12 one) said none of its presents had gone up, uncovered as covered (finding 40), and 9 of 12 keys were lost, the other 3 hidden or overtaken by none; the compositor clock walked every motion episode of the same window meanwhile. Whether they ever count on this PC: the run of 2026-11-08 19:37 saw 5 of 12 keys, so on some screens or at some times they did, and what differs isn't known (the screen the window is on, it being wider than its screen, finding 43, DWM's composition of that screen). A key's time to the screen therefore can't be had from GetFrameStatistics here, and present.ps1 says a run where Windows' statistics never moved, or where every key's window was covered, is unjudged (harness.psm1's `Set-Unjudged`, Lachlan's E5 of 2026-11-09), never a pass and not the build's failure; some keys seen and some lost still fails. The timing it rests on feeds only the budget runner's `screen_ms`, which since his 2a of the same day runs only on a speed branch. The place to look next is a frame's present id read from the compositor clock's frame statistics (DCompositionGetFrameStatistics, COMPOSITION_FRAME_STATS), which counted every episode here, in place of the swapchain's.

## Checking it

- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras counters` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package lbterm-extras`): the tables (a site claimed once, the overflow), timing runs, frames credited to the requests since the last, a window that goes, a reset that keeps what's owed, timers fired and dropped, PDUs each way, names added up, the report's JSON, the process and threads over a span, histograms. `control`'s tests read `metrics`; codec's that it notes each PDU's payload.
- `lbterm\perf\metrics.ps1 [-Mux]`: end to end in a test window, through the pipe; every wait on the state file, never a capture (PrintWindow paints the window itself). The first reply's process numbers and histograms; nothing painted, asked for or drawn at rest for 4 s; every frame the state file counts for an echo counted, with who asked; keys; a split the GUI makes itself, with its tasks, its frames' askers and, on a mux domain, the SplitPane PDU; a reset after a reset; `lbterm control metrics`.
- `lbterm\perf\metrics.ps1 -Measure [-Bin <build>] [-Mux] [-Counts]`: typing and full-speed output, GUI CPU and frames, for comparing builds.
- The harness's `Format-Metrics` and `ConvertTo-MetricsTable`, and `Start-FrameRecorder` and `Wait-Recorded` (every frame kept as it's renamed into place, the first that shows something found in order and the next wait carrying on after it, a frame no later than the last not kept, a timeout saying what the last said) and `Get-UnixMs`: `lbterm/tests/harness.Tests.ps1`.
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras budget` (and `slow`, `counters`, `hist`): a window painting at rest named by who asked as its 10 s end, a few frames not said, busy windows and what's in flight never judged, a purpose's ticks bringing their frames, timers and tasks up to its rate and the rest at rest with a note, a purpose 10% and 20% faster than it says losing only its extra ticks and one twice as fast said, a tick's window holding only so much, work at rest counted only while every window rests and never the measuring's own, a background tab's news keeping the process busy but not its window's frames and its alerts' frames allowed up to `rest_news_frames`, tasks over their time though few, the first span said at once and the rest once a minute, frames nobody asked for counted as Windows' own `WM_PAINT`, an action under its time saying nothing and one over saying what the time went on, more over in 10 s added up, untimed actions and ones never shown, a key whose program never echoes given up on without a line, the ring of recent runs seeding a late begin, off judging nothing, the totals `metrics` reports (with each action's percentiles, and a reset), the names; slow's `cause` (a press taken by the first action, a move between not a cause, one too old not one, a key that waited in the queue started from its stamp less a tick); counters' frame naming who asked, a window gone claiming no slot, running out of slots said; the histogram's buckets and percentiles, and what a record costs; `state`'s list of what plays forgetting what ended long ago; a blink's tick at its own rate, never under `animation_fps` nor over the fastest blink (`animation_rate`). `config`'s tests: `lbterm_budget`'s defaults (`rest_news_frames` among them), setting each, refusing a negative time, a reload saying when it started.
- `lbterm\perf\budget-log.ps1 [-Mux]`: end to end in a test window drawn as Lachlan's, its lines read from its log: 12 s at rest saying nothing and counting no frame or timer at rest; a clock in view (update-status each second) all on purpose; a blinking cursor at `animation_fps` 1, the window told it has the keyboard, all on purpose at the blink's own rate, nothing counted at rest and no line; a Lua timer setting the right status 30 times a second named by the site metrics names, with a line for its tasks (and one for its timers when a counted timer runs, which a frame no longer arms since perf/frame-loop), which `lbterm.ps1 logs -Budget` reads back; a tab switch after a 60 ms Lua handler said with the handler's task; every action shown and counted, with its percentiles, in `budget.actions`. `-Measure [-Runs 15]` times each action that many times, the rail and the InMail list too.
- `lbterm/tests/logs.Tests.ps1`: `ConvertFrom-LbtermBudgetLine` for every shape budget.rs writes, `Group-LbtermBudgetLines`, `Format-LbtermBudget`, and `lbterm.ps1 logs -Budget` and `-Errors` on logs written as ringlog writes them.
- `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1`: the repo's budgets.json read, every scenario in it with a setup in budgets.ps1; a scenario's budgets over the defaults; the files it refuses (a metric it doesn't measure, no max, a name twice, a domain or kind it has no setup for, no `what`, another version); `-Only` by name and wildcard; medians and percentiles; a span's numbers from two snapshots and a reply (cycles to ms, the state file's writer left out, a server's and a conpty host's, a process gone, counts over the reply's own span, a build without metrics); judging (pass at or under, over saying by how much, what a build doesn't measure not judged, no spans or a setup's failure an error, who ran added up); the report's summary, the table's rows, marks and failures, the history line and its appending; what a scenario's run left (the budget's own failures not errors, a throw an error, nothing at all). For actions: every action in the repo's budgets.json with a setup and budgets for its time, its p95 and its GUI thread, the throughputs held to a least; an action's runs, warm-ups, GUI action and budgets read, a min among them, its kind's defaults under its own; what it refuses (a metric an action doesn't measure, a max and a min both, a GUI action the GUI doesn't time, one on a scenario at rest, no runs, a scenario named for a kind, an action's metric at rest); `-Only` by kind; a run's numbers from its times, cycles, frames and metrics reply (the GUI's own time, the state file's writer left out, its WM_PAINTs), a writer's MB a second, a call timed to its return; judging on medians, 95th percentiles and the warm-up's first, a most and a least, only warm-ups an error; the actions' own table and their values in the history.
- `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only a,b|rest|action` (`-AllScripts` for every one, only when Lachlan asks): the scenarios themselves, in test windows (The budget runner, above); `perf\budgets.ps1 -Scenario <name>` one by hand.
- `lbterm\perf\font-setup.ps1`: what font setup costs, held by `metrics`' `fonts` counts in a test window drawn as Lachlan's, locked or not ("What font setup costs"); `cargo test -p wezterm-font` (now among the check's packages) for what fonts share and keep (a font taking a found face only for a character it meets, one answered between two passes, a file put in the font dirs making the fonts again), and `config`'s for what makes the fonts (the bidi settings among them) and the fallback key, `lbterm-extras`' `start` for the steps and marks; `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1` for a run's font counts read from a reply and judged.
- `lbterm\perf\fallback-faces.ps1 [-Exact]`: which face draws a character a font meets, by pixels: the regular symbols after another font met Hangul and a zoom made the fonts again drawn as with nothing before them, and after a search of the Hangul and the symbols together said against that (Which face draws a fallback).
- `lbterm\perf\gpu-lost.ps1`: a device removed at 3 s rebuilt, now with the process's shared instance and adapter forgotten and chosen again (`gpu_warm::forget`); it passed on this branch's reviewed build (the rebuild on its first try in about 1.2 s, a slow task of 588 ms on the GUI thread, which makes the instance and adapter again there).
- `term/tests/lbterm_print.rs` (in the check, `wezterm-term`'s tests): what printing allocates, counted per thread by the test binary's own allocator: a 24-bit pen's line no dearer than a palette pen's, a copy of a line of truecolour cells in a few allocations, a scanned line with links still clustered; a run of ASCII writing what upstream's printing writes (`Terminal::lbterm_print_as_upstream`, a grapheme at a time, the reference held to having written no run), whole and a character at a time: its cells, attributes, wrap marks and cursor (a wrap, autowrap off, insert mode, DEC line drawing, over a wide character, over a line held a cell at a time, past the end, margins, a link), and a combining mark joining the letter before it. With `LBTERM_TERM_BENCH=<file>`, the bench ("What printing costs").
- `lbterm\perf\scrollback-memory.ps1`: what 800,032 lines leave the GUI holding, held to 50 MB of growth, locked or not; `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only throughput*,scrollback*` the times, CPU and memory of printing.
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras recorder`: a key a thread has recorded before found without registering again, a name made at run time and labels built for each call found too, keys whose hashes collide kept apart, and what a record costs through upstream's lookup, a kept handle and no recorder (printed). `cargo test -p lfucache` (in the check since 2026-10-31): the caches' counts by name; rounds of new keys in a cache warmed in turn and a key at a time, each missing in its first round only; a model of a window's frames, a new tab's lines missing in their first frame only; upstream's decay and eviction tests with what goes now.
- `lbterm\perf\busy-pane.ps1 [-Local]`: a pane printing at full speed beside a prompt, on a mux domain of its own: each key's echo timed in the GUI and `cli get-text` of the prompt's pane timed from the server (finding 91).
- `lbterm\perf\line-cache.ps1`: a tab shown once a window's caches are full of lines seen many times lays its lines out in its first frame only, by the state file's `work.lines`, locked or not ("What the caches keep"); `-Measure` the GUI thread a frame of two panes of still lines, to compare builds. The budget runner's `repaint`: a frame of lines the window has drawn, timed and costed, none laid out again; `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1` for `lines_laid_out` read from a reply's `caches` and judged.
- `lbterm\perf\sample.ps1` (a helper): where a test window's threads spend their time, by sampling their stacks; `lbterm/tests/sample.Tests.ps1` for its names, its shares and a run against a busy pwsh.
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras present`: a present seen at its refresh, later, or overtaken, the counts wrapping; a walk counting refreshes without a present from the first present on, a present a frame late making up the frame before, one past the motion's settling ending it, frames the history lost passed over without counting them missed, a walk that sees no present measuring nothing; an episode's paints, its names and its settling extended, what it came to and its line, a covered one said as such; the totals' actions (shown, seen, hidden, overtaken, lost, the last's times filled in once it's seen), motion and cost, and a reset.
- `cargo test -p mux lbterm_procs` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package mux`): when an ask walks (FetchImmediate and a first list always, output alone never, a process started or ended in the background, one refresh at a time), a cwd read alone in the ask, the thread's next step and its pace, the job's counts never coming back to what they were and only a start or an end said as a move, the job's structures' sizes, a real job counting a program's processes as they start and end and saying so on the port, and a cwd read against a walk; localpane's what a new list changes that consumers show and a cwd not yet readable kept till it is.
- `lbterm\perf\proc-title.ps1 [-Mux]`: a pane titled by its processes in a test window: keys typed walk nothing, a program that prints nothing in the title, the rail and the strip as it starts and pwsh again as it ends, cmd's cd read alone into the folder the GUI has, the walks and reads counted from `lbterm::procs`'s lines.
- `lbterm\perf\present.ps1 [-Mux]`: end to end in a test window drawn as Lachlan's, through `metrics`' `present`: with the timing turned off (`metrics --present off`), keys and a split watched, looked at and walked not at all; then on: keys' times in order and within a few refreshes of their presents and between their post and the reply, or hidden; a split, its close, a new tab, a switch and the rail each an episode, walked with refreshes no fewer than presents, or covered with nothing counted; keys beside a writer at full speed; what it cost. It moves its window clear first when its screen has room, and says what's over it. Its pass line says only what it checked, and what it couldn't (nothing seen going up, motion to a hidden window) is said there as UNCHECKED and in result.json's notes.
- `lbterm\perf\metrics.ps1 -Measure [-Present]`: the GUI thread by its cycles too, and with `-Present` the timing to the screen on, for what it costs.
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras kept` and `projects` (what a frame laid out kept for the next, dropped when what it was laid out at changes, and counted; the folders made once answering as each ask did) and `cargo test -p lbterm-panel look` (every field of a row in one look or the other, how far along a bar is in neither).
- `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1`: the busy section read and laid under a busy scenario's own budgets (and refused without one), and `frame_ms`, `pass_ms` and `words` from a span.
- `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only busy-one-tab,busy-tabs,busy-projects-card,busy-inbox-list,busy-rail-open,busy-strip-card,busy-objectives`: what a frame costs with each open while the tab under it paints (What the cards cost).
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras link_looks` (a line looks different only when which of its own links is under the pointer changes) and `questions` (a screen lays out the same while its conversation does), and `cargo test -p lbterm-triggers` (a line's text made once finds what its cells did, and hashes the same without its trailing blanks; what's new as lines come and go, through an hour at rest, a scroll, a reply streaming on, another line where one was, a line on two rows; the hearts' paths draw the burst as it always was, and what DWM plays is each heart within a tenth of a pixel at every millisecond).
- `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only busy-one-tab,busy-lines,busy-links,busy-triggers,busy-scroll,busy-triggers-scroll,busy-split,busy-draft,busy-draft-own,hearts`: what a frame costs with links, the easter eggs' matches, a scrolling fullscreen view and a voice draft on the screen, the lines a second laid out again, and a burst of hearts' frames and GUI thread (What a line costs).
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras marks` (what a look at a shell reads, a range read whole, a scrollback read newest first a chunk at a time) and `prompts` (history.jsonl read on gives what a whole read gives, one written anew is read from its start), `cargo test -p mux lbterm_prompts` and `cargo test -p codec lbterm_prompt_rows`.
- `lbterm\perf\shell-marks.ps1 [-Local]`: a shell's 50,000 rows opened, every prompt marked within 2 s with no line fetched and no frame or task over 30 ms; output under it costing a frame no more than over 100 rows; the window made wider and, on a mux domain, a new GUI attaching, all marked again. State file, metrics and cycles only: it runs with the PC locked.
- `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only busy-shell,busy-shell-local,busy-scrollback,busy-scrollback-local,prompts-opening`: what a frame costs at a shell over 100 rows and over 50,000, and the prompt list's opening (What a shell's marks cost).
- `lbterm\perf\line-work.ps1`: keys with the pointer on no link, on one and off it lay out their own line alone, the pointer onto or off a link lays out only the line holding it, a fullscreen view scrolled 24 lines tells an lbterm-trigger handler only of the matches the lines it brings hold, a burst of hearts paints a few frames, love on the screen through 11 s at rest isn't new at the next frame, and without a handler no Lua event's task runs. State file, log and metrics only: it runs with the PC locked.
- `lbterm\perf\inbox-mouse.ps1`: the pointer moved within a row of the open InMail list, 400 posted moves, costing the GUI thread next to nothing more than with the list shut and painting nothing, and the list's focus following the pointer down its rows.
- `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1`: the runner reading `present` from a reply (its frame to the screen, its motion), a hidden window's left out and so not judged; a budget nothing measured said as `unmeasured`, in the summary and the table; the drift since a scenario's last run, from the one history every tree shares; the mux server's and the GUI's threads by name, with what threads gone by the end spent.
- `cargo test -p lbterm-extras echo` and `cargo test -p mux lbterm_wait` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package lbterm-extras,mux`): a key typed into a pane asking for no frame of its own and one to an overlay, one that scrolled back, a pane that guesses its echo and one that may not say it changed each asking for one; the parser's wait reading at once what's there, waiting its delay and asking again when nothing is, and a 3 ms wait taking 3 ms, not the tick.
- `lbterm\perf\echo-frame.ps1 [-Mux]`: a key's echo in the first frame after it, from every frame the GUI wrote: characters typed at a prompt and a SendString binding with no frame of the key's own, the mux server's answers to them said to change nothing, a key that scrolls back to the bottom and k in copy mode painting in the key's own frame. Stage 1's build fails it on every key.
- `lbterm/tests/budgets.Tests.ps1`: keys typed one after another each timed to the first frame that drew its echo (two in one frame, a row down past every key, a key no frame shows left out), and judged on their median and the 95th percentile of each run's slowest.
- `cargo test -p mux lbterm_serve` and `cargo test -p wezterm-client lbterm_start` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package mux,wezterm-client`): one server a socket (a second refused while the first holds it, a client looking taking nothing, an abandoned claim taken by the next), two clients starting at once taking turns and one stuck waited for no longer than its wait; no server said at once with no socket file and with a dead server's, a live one connected to, a server holding the socket waited for, one letting go without answering left to be started, a started server reached as soon as it listens; `config`'s names of a socket's locks however its path is written.
- `lbterm\perf\server-start.ps1`: the server started at once with no socket file and with a killed server's (its one tab), two GUIs opened together starting one, a GUI opened while a live server's socket file is gone waiting for it and starting none, a server started by hand as a GUI starts its own leaving one serving.
- `lbterm\perf\at-rest.ps1 [-Local]` and `lbterm\perf\conversation-cost.ps1 [-Local]`: what a window like Lachlan's does at rest, thread by thread (each thread's wakes and cycles, the process's I/O), and what the scroll strip's conversations and the sessions' summaries cost, against a 46 MB stand-in transcript; lbterm/AT-REST.md has every thread a GUI starts, what wakes each, and what perf/at-rest measured.
- `lbterm\perf\dwm-loops.ps1 [-Mux] [-Measure]`: a window's loops (lbterm/COMPOSITOR.md, "Loops") playing while it can be seen, held still minimized, playing again restored; `-Measure` says what DWM did meanwhile by its own numbers (the harness's `Measure-DwmSpan`: the compositor clock's frames a second and the share of 50 ms steps without one, dwm.exe's CPU and GPU), the loops shown, minimized and none, interleaved.
- `lbterm\perf\paint-cost.ps1 [-Local] [-Spinner -IntervalMs 100] [-DrawAll] [-Measure]`: what a frame costs in a window like Lachlan's, a pane printing or a spinner's line written again, with the Conductor's outlines showing (`-DrawAll`: every visual drawn in every frame, as a frame that changes them all draws them): the GUI thread's and the process's CPU a frame, the paint's parts, the visuals drawn, what each frame made, wrote and built on the GPU, and the render passes and submits it recorded and how long that took (lbterm/COMPOSITOR.md, "What a frame costs"); it fails when a frame builds a template again, frames make buffers for nothing, a frame leaves a quad stale on the GPU (`visuals.stale`), or a frame records other than one render pass a visual drawn and one submit for each eight; with `-Spinner`, when frames write more than one row's quads to the GPU; and when a new tab, a pixel more of height or another background doesn't make again every template made from it.
- `cargo test -p wezterm-term prompt_rows` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package wezterm-term`): a prompt found by its attributes (`Line::has_semantic_type`) on the rows its zones find it, on the screen and in the scrollback, a row kept a cell at a time and as runs, the prompt at its start or its end or nowhere, and a prompt overwritten by output found no more.
- `lbterm\perf\output-hold.ps1`: in a test window like Lachlan's, 16 MB printed with five OSC 133 prompts among the last of it: every frame's copy of the rows holding the terminal under 2 ms at the 95th percentile, the prompt marks' looks under 1 us a row at the median (both from `lbterm::lines` at debug), each prompt a mark of its own on the strip, and the frames of the keyboard taken away and given back copying no row anew; the output's speed said. The build before fails the copies, the looks and the frames for saying nothing.
- `lbterm\perf\claude-working.ps1`: the scroll strip against real Claude Code, talking to a stand-in for the API on a loopback port (no account used): its spinner alone reading the screen once at most, and its view followed scrolled up and through a turn's end there (lbterm/SCROLL-STRIP.md).
- `cargo test -p config gpu_blocks` (through `lbterm.ps1 test -Package config`): `GPU_MEMORY_BLOCK` and its upload half are sizes gpu-allocator takes as they are, under wgpu's default; and `cargo test -p config view_heap`: `GPU_VIEW_DESCRIPTORS` under wgpu's default and D3D12's most, the uniforms kept (`GPU_UNIFORMS_KEPT`) a quarter of it at most and room for thousands. `cargo test -p lbterm-motion uniforms_past`: past the most kept the newest half stay whatever their order, every uniform of the frame the cut falls in with them, a window resized every frame for 1,000 frames never keeping more than the most and a frame's, nor losing its last frame's. `lbterm\perf\gpu-memory.ps1 [-Measure] [-Resizes 150]`: a window drawn as Lachlan's at rest with one tab holds no private allocation over 128 MB, under 235 MB of the GPU's own memory and under 400 MB private (findings 95 and 96: main's build before them fails all three, the blocks' alone the GPU's); a window of four panes drawing every visual, resized 150 times, keeps a bind group for each place at each size (`visuals.bind_groups`) and draws on with no GPU error; with `LBTERM_TEST_UNIFORMS_KEPT=16` the oldest go, the window drawing on.
